### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Living Mountain Barrage (10m; Simple; Instant; Perilous, Withering-only; Essence 4): While touching the ground, Aton makes a withering attack against all opponents within short range, rolling once. Aton gains full Initiative only from the target who takes the most damage, +1 per additional damaged foe. Foes this attack crashes treat all earthen surfaces as difficult terrain until they’re no longer crashed. Walking Earth Entombment (5m, 3i; Supplemental; Instant; Decisive-only; Essence 3): A successful grapple gambit against a single target encases the opponent’s limbs in stone or embeds them in the ground. The grapple continues without active maintenance; Aton takes no penalties, the grapple doesn’t end if he’s crashed, and attacking him doesn’t decrease rounds of control. Aton may use his turns to apply grapple effects of his choice, or take other actions while the stone automatically performs restrain actions.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Embrace of Earth Defense (5m, 2i; Reflexive; Instant; Perilous; Essence 3): Aton encases himself in earth and stone, doubling his Parry against an oncoming attack. The shell persists after the Charm’s effects end, leaving walls that act as cover. Aton must be in contact with the ground to use this Charm.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Blessing of Aton (20m; Simple; Instant; Eclipse; Essence 4): Aton blesses a willing supplicant with slow petrifaction. The recipient doubles her effective Strength and natural soak ratings as a Charm bonus. She gains the Exalted Healing Merit (Exalted, p. 165), but becomes resistant to treatment: Medicine pools targeting her can’t exceed the healer’s highest relevant stonebased Craft Ability, and healing Charms targeting her double their costs unless they have an earthen elemental nature. Each subsequent year, the recipient gains one −0 health level (maximum +10 levels).

#### Wong Bongerok, Censor of the South

Doomcryer’s Decree (15m; Simple; Instant; Decisive-only, Eclipse; Essence 3): Aton examines the portents surrounding his target and wields them like weapons. He may pull meteors or lightning from the sky, call up a great keening from the land, or other omens appropriate to the target. Roll 15 dice for a decisive attack on a target at up to long range. The attack has raw damage of (10 + extra successes), and imposes a −3 penalty on movement actions until the end of the round.

Older recipients sleep, unmoving, for days on end. After 20 years, they turn entirely to stone, achieving an unsettling immortality: these statues still live, minds and souls permanently suspended. Powerful sorcery, another ancient elemental’s work, or potent Exalted magic could conceivably awaken one briefly — or even reverse the process.

Wong Bongerok

Censor of the South

Background: In the aftermath of the Great Contagion, Wong Bongerok assumed the position of divine censor from his predecessor, Swan Dragon. His machinations since have guided the near South’s rise to prominence; his divine interference buoys deities and civilizations with generational fertility. From his godly allies and numerous cults, Wong Bongerok extracts an empress’ ransom of prayer and favors, using heavenly authority to sustain a political machine that holds the entire South in its grip.

Much of the year, Wong Bongerok splits his time between his client deities’ heavenly estates and earthly temples. He accepts their finest hospitality while hearing reports from both the deities and mortal spies he’s planted among their cults. In the Times of Tumult, he fears deception from all sides, and cautiously seeks the assistance of amenable Chosen to secure his empire.

Censor. Should they succeed, reversing their territories’ desertification will take generations and require the redirection of fertility from Wong Bongerok’s allies, leading to famine in the near South. Equipment: Fine silk robes, scrolls of blackmail, a red jade torque (Hearthstone Amulet, Exalted p. 501) bearing a Candent Carbuncle (Exalted, p. 607). Roleplaying Hints: Wong Bongerok’s extensive spy network grants him unparalleled access to information. While arrogant, he’s generous to valued allies, offering the use of his network to those who aid him in maintaining his regime. Conversely, he’s an implacable enemy, using subterfuge and miracles to stymie his enemies. For those who earn his ire, no cruelty is beyond him.

In battle, he takes his draconic shape: A brilliant orange lizard with a wicked-fanged maw. A long green fin runs from the crest of his head down the length of his back and tail, ending with a poisonous stinger. ### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Clothed with Humanity (Exalted, p. 523)

Draconic Might (Exalted, p. 523)

Xemadea

Lesser Elemental Dragon of Wood, the Toadstool Sage Background: Seven centuries ago, surrounded by the horrors of the Great Contagion, an elemental ascended to the ranks of dragon-kind. Xemadea still bears the scars of that epidemic, her scales mottled with pox marks. Within the fog-shrouded islands that serve as her laboratory she strives to engineer a true panacea, one she can spread across all Creation.

The mold-coated sage is willfully blind to the plague she herself has become, even as her Guild-bought slaves succumb by the boatload. Mindless mushroom-folk work her mines and oversee fresh test subjects, while spores spread on driftwood to neighboring shores. Her Guild slaver, Gerel, has gained a grim reputation, buying slaves by the dozen to fuel Xemadea’s experiments; he is her only confidant, and all too eager to stoke the dragon’s paranoia whenever profitable.

Image: A great serpent of lurid colors, bulging with fungal fins and spongey toadstool scales, Xemadea’s eyes burn with white fire that betrays her obsession. In human form she is short and stout, dark skin tattooed with the psychedelic dyes that she occasionally trades.

Secrets: Xemadea’s mines, a source of a rare fungal reagents, are almost exhausted, and she must usurp nearby mines (and their villages) if her experiments are to continue. The ghost of her original summoner — her mentor and lover — wanders the isle on moonless nights, sabotaging her work in a desperate bid to forestall her madness. Equipment: Xemadea’s largest island contains a Shogunate healing temple, its resident sage mummified mid-Contagion. She wears its Health Stone on her brow; her slaves bear its brand to slow infection.

Roleplaying Hints: Though gullible from isolation, Xemadea is utterly dedicated to her cause. She cringes from conflict, keeping heavily-infected servants out of sight while less deformed guards deal with interlopers. Quick to panic, she overreacts when faced with a genuine threat, immediately adopting her dragon form and calling every infected slave in reach to her aid.

Intimacies: Defining: “Only fools can’t understand the worth of my research.”; Major: “I dislike fighting.”, Disease (Terror); Minor: “Security in secrecy”, Windstone Gerel (Complacent Trust)

to target themselves. Once per fight, unless reset by infecting a significant character or a battlegroup of Size 2+ with spores.

Mad Witch Mushroom Cloud (25m, 4i, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Withering-only, Perilous; Essence 5): Xemadea exhales a cloud of spores, rolling 15 dice as a withering attack that deals 12L damage, ignoring soak, against all enemies in front of her out to medium range. In general, she can target up to one character at close range, three at short, and five at medium. Damaged characters are exposed to Spore Rot at Virulence 5. Once per scene.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Dematerialize (60m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Xemadea sheds her earthly body, becoming immaterial as a god. This works like the Materialize Charm, but in reverse. When she reappears, her body remolds itself from a spongey cloud.

(Virulence 3, Morbidity 2, Interval: One week): This supernatural disease is contracted from long contact with Xemadea’s mushrooms. Its virulence and morbidity increase by 2 if the target consumes them. Minor Symptom: Slick fungus grows on the skin and brain. He gains the Hideous Merit. Defining Symptom: The victim’s body is a fungal mass, his brow topped by a nodding cap. He gains the Unusual Hide (3) Merit.

Za-Ishat

the Burning Blade

Background: Born in the Southern deserts, this mighty ifrit prince traveled Creation for centuries as a hero-warlord, seeking challenges befitting her mettle. But in icy Fajad, the mortal prophet Hari of Fai-Yasar turned her from her path, converting her to his faith in a transcendent God. She settled in Fajad and became a prophet herself, guiding the people toward enlightenment and union with the divine. But the leopard cannot change her spots. Za-Ishat wished to spread the Abhari faith not merely through evangelism, but at sword’s point, and her growing influence led to schism. Cast out of Fajad, she led her apostates south on crusade against neighboring city-states and peoples, but she was humbled in war against Fajad and the conquering Realm. A fallen prophet, she now roams the Northwest as a bandit-priest, leading her followers to proselytize and pillage. Image: Za-Ishat is a powerful figure, over seven feet tall, with dark skin, a hawk nose, high forehead, and long red

hair. A smoldering orange light, like heatless fire, envelops her; it’s visible for miles on clear nights, though she can dampen it to a fitful glow with concentration. Intimacies: Defining: God [Adoration]; Major: “All shall submit to the one true faith.”; “I crave challenges befitting my power.”; Her followers (Responsibility); Minor: “Those who comport themselves with honor deserve my generosity.”; Blessed Inferno (Pride)

Secrets: Za-Ishat suspects that some of Fajad’s prophets are unbelievers cynically using the creed to gather power and worship. She’s thus far refrained from sharing this for fear of harming the faith as a whole. Equipment: Red jade armor, red jade grand daiklave, Abhari texts glossed in her own hand

Roleplaying Hints: Like a banked fire, Za-Ishat conceals her passions behind a veil of calm self-control.

EX3

She’s eager to convert heroes and champions to the Abhari faith, and to find a safe place to settle her followers and evangelize to neighboring peoples. A cunning but honorable general, she adheres to the spirit of her word. She recognizes that her followers need her, but submits to personally punishing terms for their sake if

she believes her vanquisher will honor the deal. If defeated, other Abhari prophets (whether fallen or upright) focus their attention on the PCs, wary of being next.

### Ghosts

#### Dorashenje, the Wrath of Khame

### GHOSTS

The following spirits are ghosts, dead souls who have fallen out of — or rejected — the cycle of reincarnation. Some dwell in the Underworld, while others have found a way to return to Creation.

Dorashenje

the Wrath of Khame

Background: After a century of battle on the Underworld’s ashen dunes, Dorashenje has returned to Creation to lead a vengeful crusade. Horrified to see his people decimated by slave-takers, he saw once-prosperous traders in textiles and ivory had become the household servants of Zephyrites and Dynasts on the Cynosure Coast. With his descendants’ help, Shenje has begun to restore Khame territory and reforge them into a vengeful weapon aimed at Cynosure.

In life, Dorashenje was a prince of Khameland, northeast of the Zephyrite Sedgelands. He loved his husband, bore him a son, and ruled over a period of prosperity. He passed away peacefully, surrounded by loving family. Yet he awoke under the dreary Underworld sky, conscripted into a legion of the dead. It was there he learned ghostly arts, turning feral souls into weapons. Since his return, the ancestor-prince has rallied the disaffected Khame to rescue their people from Zephyrite bonds. Widely worshiped as a hero, he is yet contested by Khame elders who condemn undead rule over the living. Unperturbed by their criticism, he orders ever more ivory hunts on the region’s dwindling elephant population to fund mercenary allies in the coming war.

Image: In death, Shenje’s skin glows a beryl shade, forever anointed with red camwood powder. He’s broad-shouldered and muscled, with a clean-shaven head and a bead-braided beard. Nearly unrecognizable from life, he uses ghostly arts to express his authentic self as a trans man. Intimacies: Defining Principle: “Cruelty deserves repayment in kind.” Defining Ties: the Khame People (Responsibility), Cynosure (Hatred); Major Tie: House Cynis (Hatred), Major Principle: “My people will not be slaves to anyone.”; Minor Tie: Aaruat (Fear)

Secrets: Shenje possesses a covert ally in the Underworld: a Zephyrite Aeon, a prince of the Underworld empire of Aaruat. Dorashenje’s

patron wishes to use him to undermine the

Realm’s grip on Zephyr and turn the people

EX3

back to their ancestors; he cares little for Dorashenje’s struggle and will turn against him when it becomes favorable. Dorashenje anticipates betrayal and seeks to gain as much as possible from their alliance first. Equipment: As a ruler, Dorashenje wears ceremonial finery: a raffia cloth skirt woven by his son and gold-plated ivory jewelry. His attendants wear ritual elephant helmet-masks decorated with imported cowries. As a warrior, he dons Zephyrite burial armor and wields a wicked khopesh.

Roleplaying Hints: Dorashenje is unused to commanding frail, living soldiers. His advisors must constantly remind him of the livings’ needs. Usually sedate and unable to muster much more than feigned warmth, the subject of vengeance animates Dorashenje like nothing else, infusing him with a clarity and charisma that stirs Khame hearts.

This Charm also inflicts a −3 penalty on vision-based rolls to track or perceive Shenje and his allies. Only usable at night.

#### Jovena Oreia, the Red-Headed Bride

Jovena Oreia

the Red-Headed Bride

Background: Two hundred years ago in the Coral Archipelago, the corpse of a young woman murdered on her wedding night sank into the ocean. It drifted for days until coming to rest on the prow of an ancient sunken ship, the center of an underwater shadowland. So fierce was the bride’s need for vengeance that her ghost reawakened the Anathema vessel, raising it to the surface to terrorize the Western oceans once again. The Red-Headed Bride roams the West to sate her soul’s need for vengeance against the living, and retreats to the Underworld ocean once she’s had her fill of mortal lives. Whenever an unfortunate vessel crosses her path, she strikes mercilessly and leaves no survivors. The ghosts of those she slaughters are bound to her ship, the Crown’s Revenge, so even in death they know no peace, serving the Bride as crew and soldiers.

A hundred whispered stories pass from sailor to sailor about the Red-Headed Bride and how to avoid her: never begin a journey after dark; cast your wedding ring into the ocean if the sunset glows red on a cloudy evening; bring a talisman or recite a special prayer to sate her hunger or warn her off. One story claims the Red-Headed Bride can be summoned. With the right offerings, always including a human sacrifice, a clever thaumaturge can set her against a specific target, whom she hunts relentlessly every night they spend on the open ocean.

Equipment: At full capacity, Oreia’s ship is host to over a hundred ghosts. They may be treated as a battle group of hungry ghosts (Exalted p. 503) with Size 3, average drill, and Might 1. The ghosts are always material on the ship. Oreia herself wields a hefty, rusting broadsword which she keeps propped up by the wheel of her ship, and a water-logged flamepiece that still works. Roleplaying Hints: For all that she’s an ancient ghost bent on destroying everyone she meets Oreia has eternity on her side. She can be patient. Oreia retreats when outmatched, to seek out her target again under better circumstances. She also creates such circumstances herself by luring people in with a half-destroyed ship in need of rescue. Oreia loathes the ritual that can bind her into service, and will do everything in her power to first ensure her target is no longer a problem, then destroy the thaumaturge who bound her.

Image: In life, Jovena Oreia was a beautiful woman, short and round-faced. Her ghost’s skin is salt-stained now, her long red hair perpetually dripping wet. She wears the remains of the Coral wedding gown she was killed in, now covered in the heavy naval greatcoat she took from the captain of the first boat she ever sank. Intimacies: Defining: “I will have vengeance against the living.”; Major: The Crown’s Revenge (Love), “Freedom or Death”; Minor: The Coral Archipelago (Loathing) Secrets: The Crown’s Revenge sank during the Usurpation, along with its Solar captain. Much of its power comes from that captain’s spirit, whose death while attuned to the ship has chained her to it; she exists now as the ship itself, unable to escape her fetters or take human form. Just as vicious as Jovena from her lonely centuries beneath the waves, the two specters are more than close, but the ship has not completely forgotten the Lawgiver that she used to be.

#### Telamedes, the Ever-Vigilant

Telamedes

the Ever-Vigilant

Background: A ghost stalks the Southern dunes on an ancient patrolman’s circuit. Where he finds injustice, he punishes not only predator, but also the prey so that both may become stronger. Thus does Telamedes inflict his grim philosophy upon the world.

The scion of a forgotten tribe, Telamedes is a soldier, guardian, and philosopher who carries the creed of his ancient people like a desperate beacon in the dark: The strong do not feed upon the weak, and the weak must work to become the strong themselves. When he comes across a farmer being robbed, he slaughters the robber and burns the farmer’s crops. When he topples a slaver, he leaves the freed slaves adrift in the deep desert to live or die by their own wits.

Roleplaying Hints: Telamedes is a guardian with nothing in specific to protect, so he clings desperately to his beliefs about “the strong” and “the weak.” His methods are dispassionate, but when he punishes “the weak,” it is out of an earnest desire to teach them, train them, and strengthen them, even if his trials are often fatal. He loves to engage in battles of rhetoric and logic, sometimes even to the point of distraction, but he has centuries upon centuries of experience that show him that his philosophy works, if only by his distorted standards.

His circuit brings him through hundreds of settlements in the South every decade or so. Those who live along his route often pray to him — for salvation, for assistance, and (most of all) for him to pass by without incident. Image: Telamedes stands seven feet tall, with shimmering indigo flesh. His features are little more than blurred impressions, like a statue worn smooth by time. Though he towers over other men, he is forever slumped, as if in exhaustion or sorrow. He is pinioned with arrows and spearheads, a memorial to countless battles. Intimacies: Defining: “The strong protect the weak.”, “The weak must become the strong.”; Major: My homeland (Desperate Longing), “Kindness must never be for its own sake.”, “We must all make one another stronger.”, “I will not yield.”

Secrets: His ancient route through the South includes a number of Shadowland oases where he shelters during the brightest days of summer. Knowledge of their locations would fetch a high price in the Underworld — or provide a Deathknight with a number of valuable beachheads in Creation. The only price that could tempt Telamedes, though, is lore from his lost kingdom; its name alone could bind him into service or send him to his rest.

Equipment: Telamedes is forever dressed for war. His breastplate and greaves seem almost mundane, if not for the rich purple verdigris of Underworld bronze, and he is never found without a spear or javelin scavenged from a fallen foe.

end of the scene, it disintegrates. For an additional 4m, the weapon gains Thrown (Long) instead.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Pain Breeds Purpose (6m; Reflexive; Instant; One round; Essence 3): Telamedes’ wound penalties are instead treated as bonuses to Parry and Resolve. Sword-Stealing Flesh Technique (6m; Reflexive; Instant; Perilous; Essence 3): When he suffers damage from a decisive attack at close range, Telamedes can make an immediate Disarm gambit using (Stamina + Resistance), adding the attack’s final damage in bonus dice.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Bound by Mercy (7m, 1wp; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 4; Eclipse): When inflicting a fatal attack, Telamedes may forgo damage to instead afflict the target with a dangerous, humbling, or humiliating mission of atonement (treat this as a sanctified Eclipse oath). Thus Always to Tyrants (5i, 1wp; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 2): Whenever he successfully attacks a foe targeted by Mark the Wicked, Telamedes can roll his Initiative against the Resolve of everyone present to inflict a Minor Tie of fear and awe towards him. If his roll overcomes (Resolve + 3), targets also gain a Major Principle towards his philosophy.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Materialize (50m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 4): Telamedes may forge a body

for himself from sand and twilight. This Charm is only usable at dusk, and he automatically dematerializes at dawn. Willing Husk Possession (25m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 4): Telamedes possesses a willing member of his Cult within 100 miles. He uses their physical Attributes, soak, and health track. Every hour of possession inflicts a level of unsoakable lethal damage on the host which cannot be healed until Telamedes surrenders control.

EX3

#### Tepet Goran, Blood-Cursed Prince of Viharos

Tepet Goran

Blood-Cursed Prince of Viharos

Background: Having spent over a century as a battle sorcerer in the Tepet legions, Tepet Goran retired to his mortal father’s birthplace in the mountains of northern Medo. There he ensconced himself in Castle Arnyeko, a decrepit manse overlooking the isolated alpine town of Viharos. Secretive and reclusive, Goran often vanished into the wilderness for weeks at a time. Then, a century ago, he disappeared for close to a year; he returned swathed in funereal white, driving a hearse laden with a coffin. Terrible screams arose from Castle Arnyeko that night, and Goran’s servants were never seen again. Soon other disappearances and gory deaths haunted Viharos. Goran’s granddaughter Ragara Keles came to investigate the dark rumors surrounding her grandfather. Unlike the manse’s servants, she emerged unharmed. She’s since visited regularly, accompanied by new servants. There are always vacancies for new servants at Castle Arnyeko.

Image: Goran is the very image of a retired officer — graying, broad-shouldered, with large calloused hands better fitted to a blade than civilian pursuits. There’s something sullen and feral about him, like a thunderhead that’s about to break. When he loses control, he takes on a monstrous mien: blood-red eyes, needle teeth, elongated claws, bestial features. Intimacies: Defining: Castle Arnyeko

(Possessiveness); Major: Members of his

bloodline (Respect); Ragara Keles (Gratitude); the Solar tomb beneath Castle Arnyeko

(Merciless Guardianship); Handsome youths

(Concupiscence); Minor: Guests (Solicitude);

Occult relics (Acquisitiveness)

Secrets: Tepet Goran died a century ago.

What returned to Castle Arnyeko is his hungry ghost. As the shade of an elder Exalt — not to mention a sorcerer — he’s far more powerful, clever, and strong-willed than lesser hungry ghosts, but he cannot wholly leash his thirst for mortal blood. Ragara Keles and the Guild supply him with slaves to devour. Deep vaults underlie Castle Arnyeko. The upper vaults contain Goran’s sorcerous laboratories and undead servants. The lower vaults contain a secret Solar tomb from the Usurpation. Goran is aware of the tomb; he guarded it with numerous bound demons and sorcerous traps during his living days.

EX3

### HUNGRIER GHOSTS

Hungry ghosts that arise from the lower souls of potent humans such as the Exalted or those who’ve amassed power through ages of existence are more puissant than their common kin. A hungry ghost that’s born from an Exalt or that has spent over a century in existence might have Essence 3, a pool of eighty motes, several additional health levels, and knowledge of ghostly Charms. Strength and Stamina (or related QC dice pools) increase significantly, along with Dexterity and Wits to a lesser extent. Charisma, Manipulation, and Intelligence diminish. Combat-related Abilities remain at full strength; fragments of other Abilities may also linger. Unlike lesser hungry ghosts, it can cross lines of salt or germinated grain for a scene by spending one Willpower. Hungry ghosts of still older and mightier humans, such as First Age Solars, are stronger still.

Equipment: Blue jade daiklave, black jade reinforced buff jacket, archaic Dynastic and Medoan clothing.

Combat: Soak 16, Hardness 7 (Artifact Reinforced Buff Jacket/Clouds at Midnight: +8/7); Parry 6 (Melee); Evasion 4 (armor penalty −1); Rush 9 dice; Disengage 9 dice

Social: Resolve 4, Guile 3

Escort: Goran is often accompanied by Kiran Staghelm (see below). At his call, a Size 2 battle group of hungry ghosts (Exalted, p. 503) rises from the vaults beneath Castle Arnyeko to protect him. He may also use Nocturnal Beast Communion (see below) to call a Size 1 battle group of wolves (Exalted, p. 562) from the woods, or a Size 3 flock of raitons (Exalted, p. 570) from the sky, all bent to his will over time by his mesmeric gaze. All manner of other beings may attend Goran at the Storyteller’s discretion.

Roleplaying Hints: Goran sees himself as gracious and cultured, and greatly enjoys having guests. Ultimately, however, he cannot resist the drive to feed upon the living. He’ll flee rather than fight to the death, retreating to his entombed corpse deep in the lower vaults. Should he be destroyed, Ragara Keles attempts to uncover his fate, and to reclaim any artifacts and occult relics his slayers took.

Attributes: Strength 8, Dexterity 5, Stamina 8; Charisma 2, Manipulation 2, Appearance 4; Perception 4, Intelligence 3, Wits 6

Essence: 4

Willpower: 5

Join Battle: 10 dice

Personal Motes: 90 (10 committed)

Health Levels: −0x2/−1x7/−2x9/−4/Incap. Abilities: Athletics 4, Awareness 4, Brawl 4, Dodge 4, Integrity (Hospitality) 1, Lore 1, Melee 4, Occult (Sorcery, Undead) 3, Presence (Intimidation) 4, Socialize (Guests) 3, Survival (Tracking) 3, Stealth 3 Merits: Artifact (Thunderbird’s Cry) 3, Blood Scent, Blood Thrall, Creature of Passions, Earthbound, Hideous, Language (High Realm, Old Realm, Skytongue) 3, Manse 3, Resources 3, Unspeakable Hunger

Flaws: Obsession (Defining): “I must consume the blood of the living.”

Attack (Thunderbird’s Cry/daiklave): 12 dice (Damage 20/5); Tags: Lethal, Melee

Attack (Bite): 9 dice (Damage 19/2); Tags: Lethal, Brawl, Natural, Piercing (see Vital Essence Consumption) Attack (Monstrous claw): 11 dice (Damage 17/2); Tags: Lethal, Brawl, Grappling, Natural

New Merit: Blood Thrall

Hungry ghosts arising from Goran’s victims gain a Minor Tie of subservience toward him. This Tie can’t be altered or removed without magic.

New Merit: Creature of Passions

Hungry ghosts can’t acquire Principles by non-magical means.

New Merit: Unspeakable Hunger

Goran’s Defining Derangement of obsession with consuming living blood can’t be altered or diminished by any means.

Thunderbird’s Cry

(Blue Jade Daiklave, Artifact •••)

A relic of the First Age, this blue jade and thunderbird-bone daiklave passed from one outcaste to

EX3

another until the conquest of Medo, when Goran’s mother claimed it as spoils of war. It emits a dreadful raptor’s cry when it’s about to taste blood.

Evocations of Thunderbird’s Cry

Passive: Once per scene, flurry a withering attack and a threaten action without the usual flurry penalties. Reset by incapacitating a nontrivial opponent.

#### Cutting Wind Strike

Cost: 2m, 2i [+1wp]; Mins: Essence 2; Type: Supplemental

Keywords: Dual, Perilous

Duration: Instant

Slicing the air, Goran attacks a target within short range. At Essence 3, spending one Willpower extends this to medium range.

Clouds at Midnight (Black Jade

Reinforced Buff Jacket, Artifact •••)

Crafted by legendary armorer Jerah Lorado for Tepet’s grandson Segar, Clouds at Midnight has seen diplomats, archers, and sorcerers through many battles. Its black jade plates and vambraces are riveted to gray-dyed river dragon hide. Blue jade inlay depicts swirling clouds illumined by silvery rays from a moonsilver crescent upon the gorget.

Evocations of Clouds at Midnight

Passive: Add one die to Stealth rolls and withdraw actions in fog, precipitation, or darkness.

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Chilling Touch (7m; Supplemental; Instant; Withering-only; Essence 1): Goran’s hand or weapon flickers ethereal for a moment, ignoring up to four points of natural or armored soak.

Flying Time Technique (15m, 1wp; Reflexive; One scene; Perilous; Essence 2): Goran moves with nightmarish speed as the world seems to slow to a crawl. Treat his Initiative as three points higher than it actually is when determining turn order each round. He adds two automatic successes on any attack roll against a character that hasn’t acted yet during this round, and adds +2 Defense against attacks made by characters who act after him in turn order.

Vital Essence Consumption (—; Permanent; Permanent; Decisive-only; Essence 1): Whenever Goran deals decisive damage with a bite attack or savage action, or kills and devours an outmatched foe outside of combat (Exalted, p. 212), he regains three motes per health level of damage dealt, and heals non-aggravated damage to a −0 health level.

EX3

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Encircling Winds Defense (6m; Simple; One scene; Essence 3): Goran whips the air around him into a frenzied gale. This provides heavy cover against ranged attacks and light cover against non-ranged attacks. Rolls to contest his disengage actions suffer a two-die penalty. If Goran is crashed, the Charm ends. Mist-Body Suspiration (4m, 2i; Reflexive; One tick; Essence 4): Goran’s body disperses momentarily into mist, allowing attacks to pass through him. He gains +4 Evasion and can dodge undodgeable attacks and uncountable damage. When he successfully dodges an attack, he suffers no onslaught penalty from it, and steals one Initiative from the attacker. This Charm offers no protection against attacks that can strike immaterial targets. Once per turn. Vertiginous Stare (2m; Reflexive; Until next turn; Psyche; Essence 4): Goran’s hypnotic eyes befuddle an enemy so that her strikes go awry. She subtracts (Goran’s Guile) successes from attack rolls against him. Once per turn.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Flow Like Fog (4m; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 4): Dissolve into mist for the duration of a move action, flowing through even the tiniest openings. Waive the mote cost if Goran has used Mist-Body Suspiration since his last turn. Wings of the Gale (10m, 1wp [3i per round after the first]; Reflexive; Indefinite; Eclipse; Essence 4): Howling winds rise beneath Goran, moving him one or two range bands upward. He can continue flying with normal movement actions, moving vertically or horizontally, or hovering in place. Each turn after the first costs three Initiative. When the Charm ends, he drifts harmlessly earthward; being crashed or Incapacitated ends the Charm.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Domino of Life (1m; Simple; Indefinite; Perilous; Essence 1): Goran appears human, losing the Hideous Merit. Terminates when under stress around potential victims.

Mesmeric Gaze (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Psyche; Essence 4): Goran meets a target’s eyes to command her will, rolling a (Charisma + Presence) persuade action as though the desired task were one level lower, and doubling 8s. If his threshold successes equal or exceed her unmodified Resolve, she gains a Minor Tie of obedience to Goran, or increases an existing such Tie by one level. Once per target per scene.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Measure the Wind (5m; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Goran can discern the nature of anyone whose blood he tastes.

Nocturnal Beast Communion (5m; Simple; Until dawn; Eclipse; Essence 3): Meeting the gaze of an animal with a positive Tie toward him, Goran rides within its mind, perceiving what it perceives and instructing it as though whispering in its ear. He uses his own Perception pools but applies the benefits of its Perception-enhancing Merits.

Sky-Mantling Gloom (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Eclipse; Essence 4): Manipulate weather within (Essence) miles, creating or intensifying mundane fog, clouds, or precipitation over the course of several minutes. Roll (Intelligence + Occult). 1 success yields minor changes, such as making a damp evening turn foggy or a cloudy day overcast. 3 successes yields major changes, such as turning a clear sky overcast or light clouds into a downpour. 5 successes can gather pea-soup fog on a hot day or rouse a thunderstorm from clear skies. Once per day.

Kiran Staghelm, the Fallen Rose

While passing through Viharos decades ago, the young outcaste Kiran Staghelm heard rumor of the fiend of Castle Arnyeko. He sought to vanquish Tepet Goran, only to be seduced and devoured by the castle’s master. Now his hungry ghost remains tied to the castle and to Goran.

He retains his living appearance as a handsome youth, his waist-length emerald hair twined with flowers. But he sheds that guise to feast on the living. His true visage is white-haired and emaciated, with blood-red eyes, claws like broken branches, and a mouthful of thornsharp teeth. Intimacies: Defining: Tepet Goran (Subservience); Major: Tepet Goran (Rebelliousness); Minor: Castle Arnyeko (Home); Guests (Pity); Ragara Keles (Resentment)

### Gods and Spirits

#### Afondha, the Lady of the Well

### GODS AND SPIRITS

The following characters are spirits — beings of Essence and will. Some spirits are gods, acting as a divine representative of a place, thing, or concept.

Afondha

the Lady of the Well

Background: The well’s aquifer runs for miles underground, spanning rivers and cisterns to bring life to the land. Generations of farmers coaxed the water above ground, and rice and grains that grow nowhere else in the East paid for gilded thrones. Three principalities once waged war over Afondha’s Well, dark blood seeping through earth to mingle with the water. Now they have reached equilibrium through treaties and marriage, so each may prosper.

Afondha is a regional fertility goddess. She has scores of God-Blooded children, begotten with men and women from all three princedoms, whom she views as extensions of herself. They fill her retinue as guards, servants, spies, and — for those born of prestigious mortal lineages — ambassadors. She has no patience for rebellion or laziness, and swiftly disowns any children who refuse to play their parts. Any who actively betray her, or even speak badly about her in public, can look forward to unfriendly visits from hireling slavers, assassins, or in some cases their own siblings. The temple-manse at the heart of Afondha’s Well draws supplicants bringing offerings. They beg for fertile lands and wombs, or the more nebulous blessing of fruitful endeavors. Others worship Afondha at more distant cisterns, and her coffers fill with riches and bounties. No gold or cornucopia compares to the value of life, however, and she resents the three principalities for reaching a peaceful arrangement. She would prefer just enough bloodshed to salt the waters a touch. Image: Afondha is all luscious curves and dark bronze skin. She never wears her raven hair the same way twice. Her cheeks appear flushed, and her eyes are the blue-grey of deep water. Often pregnant, her belly shows from the first month. Her clothes and jewelry are resplendent and ever-changing, taken from the riches sacrificed to her wells. Intimacies: Defining: “Life flows from me alone.”; Major: “Protect my well at all costs.”, My Cult (Ownership), “Blood sacrifice means more than riches.”; Minor: Agu Zhano-a Tatosu, daughter by Prince Agu Zhan (Expectant Pride)

Secrets: Afondha hears everything supplicants whisper to any well that taps into the aquifer stretching out from her temple-manse, from spouses and heirs wishing for rulers’ deaths to farmers asking her to poison their neighbors’ land, and she’s not above holding it over their heads for leverage. She harbors plans to destabilize the principalities, acting through discreet saboteurs lest she offend local war gods. She offers potential agents a

EX3

myriad of rewards, including talismans and the hand of her favorite (and unaware) daughter, Tatosu. Equipment: Collar of Dawn’s Cleansing Light (Exalted, p. 601); flowing robes (Silken Armor, Exalted, p. 600); white jade headdress (Hearthstone Amulet, Exalted, p. 601) bearing three identical opaque green-blue stones, one of which is a Harvest Gem (Exalted, p. 609) Roleplaying Hints: Afondha carries herself with self-aggrandizing confidence, but also with charm and beauty. She is an excellent hostess to those she deems worthy of her attention — especially young Exalted she can call upon once they’ve grown in power — and icy cold to anyone she deems beneath her. She changes her attire and manse’s interior design on a whim, to keep guests entertained and, not coincidentally, off-balance. She never strikes a deal unless she believes she’ll come out ahead.

Attributes: Strength 3, Dexterity 3, Stamina 4; Charisma 4, Manipulation 5, Appearance 5; Perception 3, Intelligence 4, Wits 2

Merits: Artifact (Hearthstone Amulet) 2, Artifact (Collar of Dawn’s Cleansing Light) 2, Artifact (Silken Armor) 4, Cult 4, Exalted Healing, Followers 2, Language (Native: Old Realm; High Realm, Riverspeak) 2, Manse 5, Retainers (God-Blooded Bodyguard) 4, Resources 4, Selective Conception

The temple-manse’s guards use traits for battle-ready troops (Exalted, p. 496) and form a Size 2, Might 1 battle group with average Drill. Two elite bodyguards (Exalted, p. 497) accompany Afondha everywhere, tongueless and unflinchingly loyal.

EX3

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Geyser’s Distant Caress (6m, 1wp; Simple; One scene; Eclipse; Essence 2): Afondha may perform gambits against targets at up to medium range using Manipulation + Brawl with bonus dice equal to her Cult (10 total dice), as long as she’s within short range of at least a gallon of open water. Successful grapples performed with this Charm immediately pull the target to close range.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Still Waters Run Deep (5m, 2i; Reflexive; Instant; Withering-only; Essence 1): Afondha gains bonus soak equal to the number of dice that show failures in the attack roll, or double that amount if she is partially or fully submerged in her aquifer’s water. Activate this Charm before the attacker rolls.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Aqueous Rebirth (5m, 1i; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 1): Afondha briefly dissolves into water. She may automatically succeed on a disengage attempt. Alternatively, she may roll Dexterity + Cult (7 dice) while being grappled to reduce the opponent’s turns of control by the number of successes she rolls. If this reduces the turns of control to zero, she gains two Initiative.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Thirst-Quenching Goddess Regalia (8m, 1wp; Reflexive; Instant; Eclipse; Essence 3): Whenever Afondha succeeds at a bargain action or chooses not to apply her Resolve against one, she may activate this Charm to automatically instill a minor Intimacy for herself in that character with a positive context chosen by the opponent’s player, such as awe, respect, or trust. If the character already has a positive Intimacy for her, it increases in intensity by one instead. If the character already has a negative Intimacy for her, it decreases in intensity by one instead.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Fertile Benediction (15m; Simple; Instant; Eclipse; Essence 3): Afondha blesses the supplicant with fertility, ensuring conception with their next informed and willing partner regardless of whether they could normally conceive a child with that partner, as long as at least one of them has a womb.

Hurry Home (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Afondha dissolves into water on her next turn, returning to her manse’s hearthroom. Materialize (45m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Afondha solidifies from water.

Measure the Wind (5m; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Afondha can read anyone who willingly asks for her blessing.

#### Iron Tiger, the Wyrd of the Broken Sword

Vine-Ripened Labor (15m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 3): Afondha grants fruitful progress on one specific task, such as attracting a suitor’s attention or winning a negotiation with a rival. The supplicant

receives four banked bonus dice, which they can add to any one roll pertaining to the task before the lunar month ends; if that roll succeeds, the supplicant tithes one Willpower to Afondha.

Iron Tiger

the Wyrd of the Broken Sword

Background: Sandred was born an impoverished youth of the Hundred Kingdoms. Destiny demanded that he take up the sword Iron Tiger and, buoyed by his pride in the ancestral blade, seize power — first as a bandit, then a mercenary, and finally a prince whose reign would unite the region under a single banner. Yet in Sandred’s first battle, Iron Tiger shattered in his hand. But Heaven is not so easily denied. The sword’s destiny, slain by circumstance, arose in the Underworld as a wild warrior-woman with eyes like red-hot iron. She made her way into the world of the living, took up the broken hilt of her namesake blade, and set out to champion Sandred’s ascent to royalty — whether he desires it or no.

Roleplaying Hints: Iron Tiger’s first priority is protecting Sandred. Rarely leaving his side, she remains within close range in combat, using defend other as needed, unless she needs to sacrifice herself to buy time for his escape. She’s most easily manipulated by offering ways to better fulfill the destiny she represents, or threatening to make that destiny difficult or impossible to fulfill. Should Iron Tiger be permanently destroyed, leaving Sandred to perish or fall from his intended destiny, that will draw Sidereal attention.

However much Sandred’s struggles might have matured him had Heaven had its way, he now grows proud and cocksure with Iron Tiger at his right hand, living it up on stolen silver while grousing over the travails of conquest. For her part, she has no scruples and no pity; she’ll kill anyone and destroy anything to put Sandred on his destined throne. Image: Iron Tiger is short and powerfully built, with skin naturally striped rust-red and steely gray. Her eyes smolder like molten metal; few can withstand the weight of her regard. Her posture bespeaks a readiness for violence that borders on bloodlust. Intimacies: Defining: “Sandred shall seize his throne and unify the Hundred Kingdoms.”; Major: “Obstacles deserve neither consideration nor mercy.”; Sandred (Loyalty); Minor: “I serve the will of Heaven.” Secrets: Working covertly to clear a path for him, Iron Tiger has embezzled funds from the bandits’ treasury, intending to hire the Grass Spider assassin clan to eliminate powerful enemies. She blames these thefts on members of the bandit band she deems treacherous, as an excuse to kill them for Sandred’s sake. Equipment: Broken iron sword, haphazard patchwork armor, military field gear.

EX3

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Broken Destiny Forged Anew (10m, 1wp; Reflexive; One scene; Dual, Perilous; Essence 3): This Charm may only be used when Iron Tiger is at Initiative 15+ and Sandred is within close range. She and the broken sword fuse into one, a blazing tiger-striped blade that leaps into Sandred’s hand and fights with all the fervor of a destiny denied. For the rest of the

scene, Sandred adds +3 to Parry, soak, attack rolls, and raw withering damage. Iron Tiger continues to take attack and/or defend other actions while in sword form when her turn comes up in Initiative order, moving in Sandred’s hand with blinding speed. This Charm terminates if either Iron Tiger or Sandred is crashed. Wyrd’s Razor (2m per die; Instant; Supplemental; Essence 3): In Sandred’s presence, Iron Tiger may add up to three dice to any action in combat, including Join Battle.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Guardian Sword Spirit (1m [+1wp]; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 2): Iron Tiger takes a defend other action to protect Sandred while he’s in close range. If she also spends a Willpower, the attacker cannot choose to target Sandred upon beating Iron Tiger’s Parry.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Sword-and-Wielder Retreat (3m; Supplemental; Instant; Essence 2): When Iron Tiger successfully disengages she pulls Sandred in her wake. This doesn’t count as his movement action for the turn.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Burning Eye of Fury (5m; Supplemental; Instant; Essence 3): Iron Tiger adds three dice to a threaten

action against a character standing in the way of Sandred’s destiny. The target must spend an additional Willpower to resist this influence in the Decision Point.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Fallen Destiny Compass (—; Permanent; Permanent; Essence 1): Iron Tiger always knows direction and distance to Sandred.

New Merit: Wyrd

Iron Tiger is bound to the destiny she represents — Sandred claiming a throne and unifying the Hundred Kingdoms. Her Intimacy representing that destiny cannot be altered or eroded, and social influence opposing it is unacceptable. If slain, Iron Tiger returns to life at dawn to fulfill her task. However, she ceases to exist if the destiny she represents either comes to pass or becomes utterly impossible to achieve. Effects such as Ghost-Eating Technique can also slay her permanently.

Sandred, the Destined Prince

Young and handsome, Sandred’s once-rawboned frame has been softened somewhat as he feasts on the bounty of banditry, but travel and battle keep him fit. He’s

uncomfortable with his dependence on Iron Tiger, but isn’t introspective enough to understand his own role in that dependence.

Evasion 2, Parry 3

Soak/Hardness: 8/0

Intimacies: Defining: “I deserve to be a prince.”; Major: “What’s the point of power and wealth if you can’t relax and enjoy it?”; Himself (Ambivalence); Iron Tiger (Petulant Reliance); Minor: “I’ll do what I must to survive.”; His bandits (Fellowship)

Essence: 1; Willpower: 5; Join Battle: 5 dice

Health Levels: −0/−1x2/−2x2/−4/Incap

Ixier

Who Dreams for the Dead

Background: Three years ago, on the edges of a ragged Wyld patch, a gluttonous raksha feasted upon the dreams of an entire village. By the time a band of Exalts arrived to put a stop to it, the people were already empty husks. To their deep sorrow, the champions put the villagers out of their misery.

black as oil slicks. Between forms, its victims’ faces and features surface and recede in a constant churn.

The Exalts turned their grief and rage on the Fair One and killed it, with its belly still full of its victims’ dreams. Those dreams’ remnants leaked out along with the raksha’s blood, seeping into the soil of the village square. The heroes burned the creature’s body, buried the villagers, and departed for other adventures.

Secrets: When pursuing one of its victims’ dreams or goals, Ixier sometimes forgets it’s not human. It adopts the victim’s form and mannerisms, seeking out people and places from the deceased’s past to fulfill her wishes or exact vengeance.

Left alone at the center of the shadowland born from the villagers’ deaths, with the Wyld’s fingers creeping in like an infection, the alien blood and stolen dreams coalesced into something new. The entity that rose from the shadowland named itself Ixier, and declared its domain the dreams of the dying. It wanders the bordermarches, driven and haunted by its victims’ lost hopes and seeking to fulfill the wishes of those whose dead dreams gave it life. Image: Ixier mimics the shapes of dreamers it’s fed upon; one moment it may be tall and hulking, the next, wispy and childlike. Only its eyes remain unchanged:

Intimacies: Defining: “The dreams of the dead live on in me.”; Major: “I will finish that which was left undone.”; Minor: The Raksha’s Murderers (Hatred)

Ixier has consumed several Exalts’ dreams. These give it insight into all kinds of secrets — such as the location of a forgotten manse, the password to enter a secret salon in the Imperial City, or the place where a lovelorn Sidereal hid her heart. Equipment: Ixier bears weapons and armor it collects from its victims. These vary from the villagers’ common bows and hunting knives to the splendid regalia of a Prince of the Earth who died in Ixier’s domain. Roleplaying Hints: To survive, Ixier has become like the raksha whose death created it, feeding on the dreams of the dying. Easterners view it as an unwelcome psychopomp. It seeks out battlefields and places where illness

### AN ECLECTIC PANOPLY

Ixier collects its victims’ weapons and armor, and uses whichever best matches its current goals. Storytellers may assign it other equipment that fits the story, and adjust combat traits accordingly. Ixier may even wield artifact weapons it’s claimed from Exalted victims, and use their Evocations.

EX3

runs rampant — where the feasts are. In leaner times, it slips into smaller villages and settlements to sit beside the deathbeds of the infirm.

Ixier is protective of the shadowland it hails from, tending to the houses and occasionally attempting to grow crops on the land — some of the villagers had simple dreams of home and prosperity, which it values as strongly as the dreams of a champion bent on adventure and glory.

Characters who are gravely wounded (at −4 or Incapacitated, or suffering other injuries at the Storyteller’s discretion) attract Ixier’s attention. The creature responds favorably to offers to help it complete the tasks its victims never could.

The longer Ixier is left unchecked, the more formidable it becomes. To increase its threat, determine how many additional health levels it’s gained using Consume Dreams (below) in the current story, and what associated Ties it possesses, when it encounters the characters.

Willpower: 8

Personal Motes: 30

Merits: Danger Sense, Direction Sense, Fleet of Foot, Malleable Shape (below)

Attack (Self bow): 9 dice at short range (Damage 10); Tags: Lethal, Archery (Long), Mounted Attack (Spear): 7 dice (Damage 12); Tags: Lethal, Melee, Piercing, Reaching

New Merit: Malleable Shape

Ixier changes its shape at will, taking on the appearance of anyone whose dreams it has eaten.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Nightmare Torrent (10m; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 2): Ixier unleashes the horrors of the shadowland and its victims’ worst fears on its opponent. The target rolls a die and loses points of Initiative equal to the value it shows, which Ixier gains. If this crashes the target, she reflexively flees one range band from Ixier and forfeits her next reflexive move action.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Impending Death Scent (5m or 10m; Simple; Until Ixier reaches its destination; Eclipse; Essence 2): Ixier senses death approaching, and travels to meet it. It’s drawn unerringly to anyone within 50 miles who is soon to die, such as those in their final hours of an illness or old age, or bleeding out from wounds. It especially loves fresh battlefields, sick houses, and other such places. For the duration, Ixier travels at double its normal rate of speed to reach this location. For 10

EX3

motes, if mounted, it may double its mount’s speed instead.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Banquet of Daydreams (8m, 1wp; Supplemental; Instant; Essence 2): On a successful attack, Ixier feasts on its target’s dreams. A target with an Essence pool loses five motes (personal first, for Exalts). Ixier gains a Minor Tie to the subject of any one of the target’s Ties (player’s choice). The victim loses the Tie until Ixier dies, or she convinces it to return the dream, which it can do as a miscellaneous action. Ixier gains a −2 health level for each Tie it collects this way. Once Ixier takes damage to one of these health levels or gives back the dream that provided it, that level vanishes permanently. Once such a health level vanishes, the dream is gone and cannot be returned. At the end of the current story, Ixier fully digests and absorbs any dreams it still holds, losing all but one of the health levels and Ties this Charm bestowed. Hurry Home (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 2): Ixier fades away on its next turn, returning to the shadowland where it was born.

#### Ninegala, Forge-Goddess of Makelo

Ninegala

Forge-Goddess of Makelo

Attributes: Strength 7, Dexterity 5, Stamina 7; Charisma 4, Manipulation 2, Appearance 4; Perception 6, Intelligence 5, Wits 5

Essence: 5

Willpower: 8

Join Battle: 9 dice

Personal Motes: 100 (10 committed)

Health Levels: −0/−1x7/−2x7/−4/Incap.

Abilities: Athletics 3, Awareness 4, Brawl 3, Bureaucracy 3, Craft (Armorsmithing) 7, Craft (Artifacts) 7, Craft (Weaponsmithing) 7, Dodge 3, Integrity 4, Linguistics (Old Realm) 3, Lore (Geology, Metallurgy) 4, Melee 5, Medicine (Treating Burns) 2, Occult (Fire Spirits) 3, Presence 3, Resistance (Heat and Flame) 6, Ride (Automata) 4, Socialize (Makeloans) 1, Thrown Merits: Artifact (Boundless Javelin Quiver) 3, Artifact (Flying Bronze Chariot) 4, Artifact (Forge-Mother’s Hammer) 3, Artifact (Ur-Golem Armor) 3, Cult 4, Five-Thousand-Year Panoply (below), Language (Native: Old Realm; Seatongue) 1, Legendary Artisan (below), Scripture of the Forge (below) Attack (Forge-Mother’s Hammer): 13 dice (Damage 19/4); Tags: Bashing, Melee, Smashing Attack (Javelin): 13 dice at short range (Damage 16); Tags: Lethal, Melee, Piercing, Thrown (Short) Combat: Soak 18, Hardness 10 (ur-golem armor: +11/10); Parry 6 (Melee); Evasion 2 (ur-golem armor −2); Rush 6 dice; Disengage 6 dice Social: Resolve 5, Guile 2

Guardians: Numerous golems (Exalted, p. 500) protect Ninegala’s vaults, alongside various other automata and traps.

Background: Ninegala once labored at the forge of one of the world’s makers. When the Exalted cast those ancients down, she exiled herself to Creation, raising a sanctum on the Western isle of Sinti. Eventually, an enterprising mortal hero persuaded her to forge him a weapon in exchange for a year of service; others trickled in over the centuries. During the Contagion, she opened her doors to a tribe of survivors. In gratitude, when they founded the city Makelo (Exalted, p. 105), they took her as their sovereign god.

Image: Ninegala has charcoal-black skin, ash-gray hair, and fiery eyes that blaze when her passions are aroused. She wears little in the forge’s heat, but when traveling abroad, she arrays herself in arms and armor of her own devising as she takes up her flying bronze chariot’s reins. Intimacies: Defining: “I wish only to craft wonders in peace.”; Major: “I enjoy testing my art’s limits.”; Makeloans (Affection); Minor: The Fair Folk (Loathing) Secrets: An ancient goddess, Ninegala holds faded memories of the Time Before — specifically, the divinity she served and his workshop in Heaven — though she’s forgotten more than most living gods recall. She retains a few relics from that era in her vaults, hidden in plain sight where none but she might recognize them. Equipment: Forge-Mother’s Hammer (Goremaul), UrGolem Armor (see below), Quiver of Boundless Javelins (see below), aerial chariot

Roleplaying Hints: Those seeking Ninegala’s impossible marvels must perform long, weird, and perilous quests — both to obtain rare crafting materials, and to discourage future petitioners. Characters might persuade her to lend her services without such quests if the desired artifact is sufficiently exotic and intriguing, or to ward off a potential threat to her or Makelo’s people. Should the characters destroy her — to loot her armory, perhaps, or claim Makelo and its forges for themselves — powerful beings across the West descend on her vaults. Some assume the characters have already claimed the cream of Ninegala’s arsenal and track them down. In addition, Ninegala retains a few connections in the spirit courts, such as the dragon Fakharu (Exalted, pp. 521523); these may aid her in retrieving stolen goods, or avenge her if she’s destroyed.

New Merit:

Five-Thousand-Year Panoply

Ninegala may find any simple, straightforward two- or three-dot artifact within her vaults. Once per story, if she requires an artifact with specific, esoteric powers, roll (Essence) dice against the artifact’s rating. Success reveals it in her vaults; failure reveals an artifact of similar function whose rating only equals her successes on the roll.

EX3

New Merit:

Legendary Artisan

Ninegala doesn’t track or gain crafting experience — if she must pay a crafting experience cost, she’s always able to meet it. She has ten major project slots and five superior project slots.

New Merit:

Scripture of the Forge

Students learning Craft from Ninegala halve training times. They gain +2 soak and Hardness against fire-based attacks and immunity to minor burns, extending afterward for a duration equal to the training time. Students who spend years training under Ninegala obtain fiery eyes and charcoal-dark skin, marking them as masters of the forge.

Boundless Javelin

Quiver (Artifact •••)

An oblong case of teak and darkened bronze, inlaid with the Old Realm word for “Plenty” in orichalcum and moonsilver, contains a dozen iron javelins.

Evocations of the Boundless

Javelin Quiver

Passive: The attuned quiver never runs out of javelins; new ones appear to replace the old.

#### Distant-Striking Dart

Keywords: Uniform

Duration: Instant

Attack a target at up to long range, using the same dice pool as short-ranged javelin attacks. Spend one Willpower to attack at long range without an aim action.

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Smoldering Metal Offensive (10m, 1wp; Simple; One scene; Uniform; Essence 3): Superheat a weapon Ninegala wields, adding +4 to withering and decisive damage. A mundane weapon burns to ashes or melts to slag after this Charm ends.

EX3

Fire Knows the Forger (5m; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 3): Add (Essence) successes to resist a fireor heat-based environmental hazard and subtract that many dice from its damage. Against attacks from sources such as firewands or fiery dragon breath, this Charm instead halves their damage.

Steel Knows the Shaper (5m, 2i; Reflexive; Until next turn; Essence 3): Gain +1 Defense against weapon-based attacks. If a character attacks Ninegala and misses, he’s automatically disarmed — the weapon flies from his hand lest it strike the forge-goddess. Unarmed attacks and improvised weapons are unaffected.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Forge-Mother’s Grace (1m+; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 4): Using an item she made herself, Ninegala adds bonus dice as with a Solar Excellency (Exalted, p. 255). Her maximum is based on the level of project used to create the item — Basic: +2,

Major: +4, Superior: +6, Legendary: +8. Hammer-and-Anvil Genesis (25m, 1wp;

Simple; One project; Stackable; Essence 5): Automatically succeed on the roll to complete a basic or major project. Superior and legendary projects instead double 7s, double extra successes received at each interval, and add five rolls to the terminus. This Charm can stack to benefit multiple projects. Hurry Home (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Ninegala fades away and vanishes on her next turn, returning to her sanctum.

Materialize (50m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Ninegala appears in a wreath of flame. #### Resplendent Twin,the Shining-Heeled, Queen of Reflections

Resplendent Twin

the Shining-Heeled, Queen of Reflections

Background: Once the celestial goddess of heliographs, Resplendent Twin lost much of her power when the chaos of the Contagion and the Fair Folk invasion destroyed the Shogunate heliograph network. When the Realm assembled its own system, a better-connected subordinate stole her position. Piqued, she left Heaven for the Scavenger Lands, establishing a cult as a goddess of messengers, merchants, travelers, and good fortune. Formerly a grand dame in the Celestial City’s cotillions, she misses Heaven’s high society, traveling thousands of miles to attend particularly lavish soirées or pursue whispers of reinstatement in Yu-Shan. She favors invitations from wealthy socialites and grants the richest social contacts, confidential information, and occult benisons. The nature of their business — whether mercenary work, drugs, or slavery — concerns her not at all. Image: Resplendent Twin appears as a tawny-skinned woman with smoke-colored hair, clad in tunic and skirt of silver, gold, and bronze, or an elegant gown woven of

light. Her body glows when illuminated, shining ruddily in firelight or blazing beneath the sun. When she runs, sparks fly in her wake. More often, she projects her image into priests’ and petitioners’ mirrors. Intimacies: Defining: Herself (Vanity); Major: “I miss Heaven’s luxuries.”; “Knowledge is power.”; “Money has no provenance.”; “Returning to Heaven would be an admission of defeat.”; Rodo-Capiri, Goddess of Heliographs (Bitter Grudge); Minor: “I respect a good host.”; Great Forks (Affection); Her devotees (Obligation) Secrets: Greyfalls’ Cynis households regularly invite Resplendent Twin to their galas. In exchange, she funnels information and business to them. Meanwhile, she remains willfully ignorant of her priests’ efforts to sabotage messengers and merchants who don’t tithe to the cult.

Equipment: Gleaming garments, jewelry, mirror-bright silver sword, blazing sunbeam bow

EX3

Roleplaying Hints: Resplendent Twin desires wealth, power, adoration, and pleasure. She’s not malicious, but cares little who’s harmed in acquiring such things. Confronted about collateral damage by incensed Exalted, she’ll comply with reasonable requests, but eventually backslides. (She understands Eclipse oaths and avoids unequivocal promises.) She flees from danger, abandoning everything to avoid death — and waiting to rebuild her power before seeking revenge. Ending her or her cult results in economic upheaval; her dependents fall from power, others rising in their place.

Essence: 4

Willpower: 7

Join Battle: 11 dice

Personal Motes: 90

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Incandescent Reflection Mantle (15m, 1wp; Reflexive; One scene; Eclipse, Uniform; Essence 4): Resplendent Twin draws in ambient light, enfolding herself in a shimmering aura. It’s merely cosmetic in low-light conditions, but otherwise imposes visual penalties on any character attacking her or defending against her attacks, up to −3 under open daylight or inside a well-lit structure. Especially bright light suffuses her weapons with reflected radiance, adding (Essence) to the raw damage of withering and decisive attacks.

EX3

Tower-to-Tower Arrow (7m, 1wp; Supplemental; Instant; Decisive-only; Essence 4): Resplendent Twin nocks and fires an unblockable arrow of reflected light out to extreme range. It can bank off reflective surfaces to bypass cover — even full cover, if she knows her target’s location. Each bank shot can extend its range by an additional extreme range band.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Shard Twin Flash (10m, 4i; Reflexive; Instant; Perilous; Essence 4): Resplendent Twin sheds a luminous reflection between her and an attacker. It performs a defend other action using her Parry. If the attack hits Resplendent Twin, she waives this Charm’s Initiative cost the next time she activates it this scene.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Mile-Eating Stride (15m, 1wp; Simple; One day; Essence 3): Resplendent Twin runs tirelessly at 100 miles an hour, crossing impassable terrain such as lakes or canyons up to a mile wide by vanishing and reappearing on solid ground. Double 7s on movement rolls, moving from point to point in combat without crossing the space between, ignoring hazardous terrain. With a touch or to answer a prayer, she may bestow a lesser form of this Charm. The recipient gains the same speed and endurance, but cannot cross through space and only doubles 9s on movement rolls; the duration becomes Indefinite, lasting until he’s stopped moving for more than a scene.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Everything-in-Common Attitude (15m, 1wp; Reflexive; One scene; Essence 2): After a few minutes interacting with a character or observing him in a social setting, Resplendent Twin makes a read intentions roll to mirror his demeanor and mannerisms, ingratiating herself to him. If successful, treat him as having a minor positive Tie towards her and add +2 to her effective Appearance against him (even above 5.) She conceals her own motives while reflecting his, adding +2 Guile. If a character fails to read her intentions but rolls enough successes to beat the Guile of this Charm’s target, he perceives her as holding the intentions or Intimacies of this Charm’s target.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Hurry Home (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Resplendent Twin becomes two-dimensional and vanishes on her next turn, reappearing in her mirror-filled Great Forks sanctum. Materialize (45m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Like a mirage, Resplendent Twin steps forth from momentary heat-haze.

Measure the Wind (5m; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Resplendent Twin can discern the nature of anyone she sees in a reflective surface.

#### Siakal, Western God of Battle, Slaughter, and Sharks

Mirror-as-Window Method (5m; Simple; One

scene; Eclipse; Essence 4): Resplendent Twin projects her appearance into a mirror within (Essence x 10) miles, allowing her to see out of it and converse through it. She may invoke this Charm reflexively to answer a prayer to her at an extended range of

(Essence x 100) miles. Once per story, after she’s appeared in a mirror, she may pay an additional 45 motes and a Willpower to step out of it, appearing on the other side. Eclipse Castes who learn this Charm cannot use it to answer prayers.

Siakal

Western God of Battle, Slaughter, and Sharks

Background: When bloody skirmishes turn Western waters crimson, the mighty Siakal smiles, her maw full of pointed teeth. The fiercest battles are feasts for her many children, those sharks and siaka who catch the scent of blood on the waves and gorge themselves on sailors who go down with their ships.

The battle-god spends much of her time in Yu-Shan, swimming in a great pool of black basalt. She rarely involves herself in politics, unless Heavenly debates touch upon wars in the West. It does little good to threaten other war gods with conflict when Siakal’s aims clash with her peers’, so she’s learned other cutthroat tactics to ensure her will prevails. This doesn’t mean she never resorts to violence — her long and bloody disagreement with the god Tansang ended in his sacred island of Fenua sinking beneath the waves. The bodies of its doomed inhabitants fed a thousand siaka. Though worship of Siakal is banned in Wavecrest and some other western nations, many sailors still make offerings when their ships raise battle flags. Lintha pirates wear shark-tooth amulets in her honor. In Coral, cults dedicated to Siakal turn out fierce, bloodthirsty warriors. Cautious captains wisely steer clear of vessels flying shark tooth flags — the emblem warns those who approach that the crew will grant enemies no mercy.

Sharks follow in worshipers’ wakes. For a

proper sacrifice — usually ship’s captives — they’ll come to the crew’s aid in battle. However, past propitiation doesn’t ensure future cooperation. If Siakal’s children find the offerings lacking, they’re happy

to feast on the crew.

The war god has little use for bravery.

A coward can

slit an enemy’s

throat as easily as a hero can;

Siakal only cares

EX3

that blood tinges the waves. Death and destruction matter far more to her than boldness and heroics. Many a worshiper has learned that lesson only when her children were circling. Image: Most of the time, Siakal is a massive shark, stretching more than 20 yards long. In her human form, she’s an imposing woman with slate gray skin and flat lidless eyes. Rows of shark’s teeth fill her mouth. Intimacies: Defining Principle: “Battles that end in bloody slaughter please me.”; Major Tie: Children of Siakal (Motherly Fondness); Minor Principle: “My opponents will bend to my will one way or another.”; Minor Tie: The West (Territorial Ownership) Secrets: While Siakal isn’t invested in the Realm’s looming civil war, her agents bring her word of the increasing tensions between Peleps and V’Neef in the West. She’s begun quietly undermining other war gods’ positions so she can reap the majority of the benefits should the Great Houses go to war.

Equipment: Shark’s Tooth, a serrated sword made of enchanted orca bone and the teeth of dead spirit sharks; The Predator’s Hide, sharkskin buff jacket Roleplaying Hints: Siakal doesn’t bother making friends. Cool and aloof to those who seek an audience with her, she’s quick to sense weakness and seize upon advantages.

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Boundless War-God Wisdom (1m+; Reflexive; Instant; Perilous; Essence 4): Siakal may add dice to any action in combat. She cannot add more dice to a roll than her current Initiative, to a maximum of 10. Join Battle rolls prior to combat may always have up to 10 dice added.

Breaching Slam (3m; Supplemental; Instant; Uniform; Essence 3): The god drags her enemy out of the water with a mighty leap, crushing him beneath her bulk as they fall back into the waves. Siakal’s withering and decisive slam attacks also inflict falling damage on her target (Exalted, p.232). Pounding Surf Strike (6m, 3i; Simple; Instant; Essence 3): Siakal thrashes about mightily, battering her enemies with the force of the tides. The rough waters around Siakal create an environmental hazard applied against all enemies within medium range. The hazard deals 4B/round, with a difficulty of 3 to resist. Rending Essence Bite (3m; Supplemental; Instant; Decisive-Only; Essence 3): Siakal’s devastating bite tears her enemy’s flesh. Her decisive attack deals aggravated damage.

War God’s Frenzy: (5m; Reflexive; Instant; Decisive-Only; Essence 4): Bloodied waters drive Siakal to greater heights of violence. After a successful decisive attack, Siakal may immediately make a second attack against the same target.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Churning Waves Defense (3m; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 3): Siakal twists away from enemy attacks; what blows land, her thick skin turns aside. Add (Essence) dice to her Evasion or Parry.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Riptide-Riding Method (3m; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): While underwater, Siakal propels herself forward with a mighty flick of her tail, moving an additional range band in any direction.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Fanning the Flames (5m; Simple; One Scene; Essence 2): The god of battle stokes enmity from a smolder to a blaze. Siakal doubles 9s on social influence actions involving her target’s negative Intimacies. If the subject of that Intimacy is present in the scene, she doubles 8s.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

General’s Prerogative (20m; Simple; Instant; Essence 3; Eclipse): Once per scene, Siakal summons

#### Yetunda Clatterbones, Carrion Queen

a fogshark (Exalted, p. 501) to appear automatically at her side. Hurry Home (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Siakal fades away and vanishes on her next turn, reappearing in her basalt pool in Heaven.

Materialize (60m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Siakal appears in a wave of blood and brine. Measure the Wind (5m; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): Siakal can take the measure of anyone whose blood she’s smelled on the wind or tasted in the water.

Yetunda Clatterbones

Carrion Queen

Background: Once a pitiably obscure gambling deity, Yetunda used souls who died over dice to buy her way to the position of carrion queen. Ever-voracious for power, she became a back-alley divinity of everything from fermentation to funerals, and as recently as a decade ago seemed on the verge of becoming a regional force. These ambitions ended when her daughter, Chausa, was consumed by the contaminated power of an Exaltation Yetunda stole. When Yetunda emerged from a years-long fugue, her cults and temples had been stripped away.

Yetunda still has one living child: Mwaka, leader of the Graveyard Kettle (see p. XX). Unlike his half-sister, Mwaka never put Yetunda on a pedestal; the two trade remarks whenever she visits, even as they share a “burnt offering” of ox-dragon steak. She often travels with Mwaka and his hunters, offering wisdom and playing the cynic. When a rare bright mood takes her, she guides them to hunt behemoths sanctified in the name of those loathsome gods who stole from her, and both she and the hunters grow strong feasting on this sacred flesh. Image: Yetunda takes the shape of a vulture with terracotta-red feathers and an oversized, wickedly-hooked black beak. In human form, she becomes an unhealthily thin woman in a sweeping feather cloak, bald head concealed by a bird-mask of woven red leather strips. Her dark, wrinkled skin is a painted canvas of red dots and lines.

Intimacies: Defining: “Don’t gamble what you’re not prepared to lose.”; Chausa (Ceaseless Guilt); Major: Mwaka (Poorly-Concealed Affection); Minor: “I miss the game.”

Secrets: Yetunda once played a game of

dice in which Mwaka won “his life” from

her. She’s recently come to suspect that, should he die, her divine essence will

be burned out to revive him as one of

the Liminal Exalted. She hasn’t told

him, finding some cold solace in the

possibility.

EX3

Equipment: Yetunda’s one remaining symbol of office is a one-handed warclub of divine bone, its bulbous head topped with a black jade “beak.” In both forms, she also wears a necklace hung with carved fangs and tusks from myriad creatures.

Roleplaying Hints: Yetunda is a worn-out old woman with a wealth of badly-earned experience and a morbid sense of humor. She guides the Graveyard Kettle as best she can, afraid of reclaiming her lost power but nonetheless drawn to do so. The prospect of her death no longer bothers her, but if she can quell an opponent with nothing more than crippling injuries and ghoulish prophecies, so much the better. Her daughter’s death weighs on her conscience; whenever she remembers the price of her hubris, she falls into a dark mood from which only Mwaka can extricate her.
