### The Abyssal Exalted

#### The Bleeding Lily Crowned in Shackles, Underworld Queen

### THE ABYSSAL EXALTED

The following characters are Abyssals, champions of the Deathlords whose powers rival the Solar Exalted.

The Bleeding Lily

Crowned in Shackles

Underworld Queen

Background: Once, there lived a princess without a care in the world. The youngest of six children, her parents sent her away from their tiny Eastern kingdom to soak in the culture of Nexus. There, she spent their money, wrote poetry, and occasionally remembered to broker a trade deal or shore up an alliance. She loved and laughed often, and dreamed of nothing more than a life spent in continued, carefree luxury.

In one night, her life was upended, then cut short. A messenger came from her parent’s kingdom: her family was dead, their throne usurped by an ambitious neighbor. Within hours, an agent of the usurpers found her and cut off their master’s last loose end with a knife to the princess’ back.

The Bleeding Lily Crowned in Shackles rose a pale shadow of herself, plagued by dark whispers. She was a queen without a kingdom, and the voice told her of a kingdom without a queen. It led her to the Underworld, across a dark ocean to an ancient castle. She took its onyx crown not out of greed, but out of obligation; if death called her to lead, then her life of joy died with her. She became the queen the dead required.

Under her rule, the Onyx Kingdom has thrived. Her laws champion fair dealings and noblesse oblige, with the strongest ghosts encouraged to support weaker specters. Citizens don’t rely on the living for happiness and fulfillment — the Lily has implemented public support and funding for artistic endeavors made by the dead, for the dead. Society frowns upon close relationships with the living, while applauding those who seek contentment within the Underworld as good examples. Despite her isolationist ideals, the Bleeding Lily is also willing to seek justice from the living for her subjects, traveling throughout the East to avenge broken promises and defiled graves. She considers herself a fair and just

deathknight: she extracts the truth from the accused before acting, and doles out punishment as befitting of the crime and intent. An honest mistake might mean a new scar, to encourage acting with more care in the future. Malicious destruction of an honored gravesite merits dragging the criminal back to the Underworld, where the victim can extract vengeance in person. The Lily isn’t merciless; true contrition earns her leniency, though she marks the guilty with the power of her Anima to ensure they keep their promises of restitution. Although the Lily tries to keep the Onyx Kingdom out of international politics, there’s no avoiding the specter of Thorns not far from her borders. She hosts the Mask’s diplomats with charm and grace, but avoids a formal treaty. The whisper that led her to the Onyx Kingdom was not from the Mask of Winters, and the debt she owes the Deathlord who gave her a throne has yet to be repaid. Image: The Bleeding Lily Crowned in Shackles is a tall and striking woman with pale skin, bone-white hair cropped short, and vibrant hazel eyes that seem red in the right light. She dresses in silk shirts and leather pants, always the finest quality but never ornamented or embroidered. At rest, her face seems tinged with a subtle grief. Intimacies: Defining: “A monarch must be dutiful and honorable.”; The Onyx Kingdom (Protective); Major: Marigold (Love); “Death is a higher state of being.”; Minor: Fine poetry (Admiration); “Protect the weak.” Secrets: The Bleeding Lily has a mortal lover, a shepherd named Marigold living peacefully in the Scavenger Lands. She believes the Lily to be a ghost-blooded hero, wandering the land and saving innocents in distress, just like she saved Marigold by slaughtering the bandits who plagued her town. The Abyssal keeps Marigold a secret, fearing what she might betray to save her maiden fair if someone got their hands on her.

EX3

Equipment: The Bleeding Lily wears the solid onyx stone crown that came with her Underworld castle. It grants her black glass armor that covers her completely, allowing her to ride into a village as a vengeful Deathknight one evening, and walk among its people as a simple traveler the next. On long journeys, she’ll often purchase and ride a black horse, though living animals in her care seldom last long. Roleplaying Hints: A knight among Deathknights, the Bleeding Lily is noble, romantic, and deeply devoted to the oaths she swears and people she protects. She deceives by omission, walking among the living without displaying her ties to the dead, but she dislikes lying outright, and loathes dishonorable combat. She believes pointless slaughter cheapens death; the living should be let be unless they have committed some crime, in which case they should be destroyed so utterly that their spirits fail to rise again.

Charms

The castle of the Onyx Kingdom is a powerful manse, its Crown a powerful artifact. Both are interconnected, and bound to the Underworld kingdom. The Crown itself is a black onyx coronet with thirteen rough points, their facets irregular and sharp, with one hearthstone socket below the tallest point.

The Bleeding Lily knows all four charms possessed by the Wandering Deathknight quick character (Exalted, p. 550) as well as the following:

Evocations of the Onyx Crown

Passive: The Onyx Crown provides control over the Onyx Kingdom, a sizable Underworld dominion. Ghosts within the kingdom gain a +1 non-Charm bonus to all actions they take under the Bleeding Lily’s direct orders, and must pay a point of temporary Willpower to break any law she sets. The Lily is instantly aware when and where a living being enters her dominion, though she gets no further details about their nature or identity.

#### Don the King’s Glass

Cost: 5m.; Mins: Essence 2; Type: Simple

Keywords: None

Duration: Indefinite

When the Bleeding Lily commits to a quest on behalf of her kingdom or a subject, she may activate this charm to summon a set of full plate armor and a broadsword, both made of black glass and with comparable strength to artifacts. She may don or dismiss these tools at will, but cannot end the charm until her quest has been fulfilled and she has returned to her castle. If this becomes impossible, the tools immediately materialize and shatter, inflicting 3 lethal damage to the Bleeding Lily.

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Vengeful Riposte (4m, 1wp; Reflexive; Instant; Counterattack, Withering-only): The Lily makes a Melee counterattack that occurs after her opponent’s attack result, but before damage has been rolled. This attack acts as a special withering attack, with successes on the damage roll reducing the target’s Initiative. Rather adding points to the Abyssal’s Initiative, convert them immediately to decisive damage dice to roll against the target’s health track, ignoring Hardness. Soul-Cutting Argument (3m; Reflexive; Instant; Decisive-only, Psyche): The Lily rolls her decisive damage with double 10s, ignoring Hardness. For each point of damage she would inflict, the Lily can instead reduce the intensity of an Intimacy she’s aware of in her opponent by one degree. If she targets a Minor Intimacy in this way, that Intimacy is destroyed. Only one Intimacy can be targeted per use of this Charm, with remaining levels of damage applied to the Health Track as normal. This Charm can be defended against via Charms and other magical means.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Truth of the Grave (3m, 1wp; Simple; Instant): The Lily asks a single question of one target. If he refuses to answer or answers truthfully, nothing happens. If he answers with a lie, her target immediately takes one lethal level of damage, which takes the form of obvious and immediate bleeding from the eyes, nose, and ears.

Dread Lord’s Demeanor (6m; Simple; One Scene; Keywords): Characters must pay a point of temporary Willpower to speak against her or attack the Lily, paid once per scene. All threaten actions against her suffer a penalty equal to her Essence.

#### The Melkin Fool in Red and the Circus Moribund

The Melkin Fool in Red

and the Circus Moribund

Background: Once, perhaps, the jingling harnesses heralded joy and wonder trundling into town. Once, perhaps, the caravans’ green paint was the emerald of a spring meadow, rather than a sickly pale yellow-gray. Maybe once the laughter spilling from the audience was mirthful; now it only sounds like screams. When the Melkin Fool in Red and the Circus Moribund set up their stages and their tents, they’re not the entertainment.

You are.

Who the Melkin Fool might have been before is long forgotten. Now is what matters to her. She’s a Day Caste Abyssal, the loyal servent to the Deathlord The Lover Clad in the Raiment of Tears, and the leader of the Circus Moribund — a troupe of strange performers, not all of whom are living… or human.

Desolation. The weapon was a gift from the Lover Clad in the Raiment of Tears, in recognition of loyal service. The braided leather cords have been dyed blood-red to match the Fool’s preferred colors.

Roleplaying Hints: The Melkin Fool is always amused. Even when she’s furious or in the midst of battle, a smile plays about her lips. Fiercely loyal to her lady, she cannot be swayed into betrayal. Characters who can explain how their plans will benefit the Lover, however, may win the Fool’s cooperation — at least temporarily. Clever wordplay delights her, even better if it’s used to jibe at someone else. The Melkin Fool has little concern for other Abyssals, which makes her enmity towards the deathknight known as The Shatterer of the Way remarkable.

Only the unwise dismiss the Melkin Fool as mere comic relief. She whispers secrets in her lady’s ear, telling the Lover who is working against her, which sycophant pledges loyalty one moment, then rushes off to seek favor with Walker in Darkness. If something is amiss in the deathlord’s territory, it’s up to the Melkin Fool to inform her — bluntly and without prevarication. The Fool is insightful and sharp-witted. Her searing remarks have made even the most carefully composed warriors falter. Image: The Melkin Fool is a lean woman who walks with an acrobat’s assured gait. She wears a blood red leotard and often covers her face with a porcelain mask whose features shift to exaggerate her mood. She carries an alarming number of knives, and is rarely, if ever still. Intimacies: Defining: The Lover Clad in the Raiment of Tears (Loyalty); Major: “It’s all for nothing. Let me show you.”; Minor: The Circus Moribund (Duty), “Nothing says pointlessness has to be so dreary.” Secrets: Several years ago, the Melkin Fool took pity on a servant in the Fortress of Crimson Ice and set him free without their mistress knowing.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

She weaves words of concern regarding that person or concept, instilling a sense of worry and fear in her target: that something horrible has happened to the person, that they’ve betrayed him, or that his beliefs were founded on false pretenses.

The Circus lures villagers in with a promise of light-hearted entertainment to break up the desolate winters, but the show they put on highlights the pointless repetition of the audience’s lives, the smallness of their existence, and the inevitability of death. Such shows often drive spectators toward despair, reckless actions, suicide, and, of course, death.

The Prince also leads a Size 1 battle group of zombies (Exalted, p. 502) with poor Drill and Might 1.

Background: The oil lamp flickered, sending shadows racing across the dusty burial chamber. The Prince Resplendent frowned and dipped his brush into the inkstone. He wrote: “As cherry blossoms fall upon the river / And are swept downstream to the sea / So do all relics of ancient days / Flow to the vaults of fallen Thorns / Seek ye there the treasures of your past.”

The Prince smiled. A shame he wouldn’t be here to see their faces, but better to be gone with the tomb’s orichalcum panoply before its rightful owners arrived. Image: Thin, sallow, and balding, the Prince Resplendent can’t quite shed the air of the shopkeep and scavenger he once was. In public, he favors wealthy garb or artifact armor complete with mask or helm, and affects a cultured tone — though surprise causes him to slip back into the lower-class accent of his youth. In private, he favors well-worn, practical garments.

Intimacies: Defining: “I’ll turn a profit on every task.”; Major: “I take pleasure in mocking my adversaries.”; “None shall witness my fears.”; The Mask of Winters (Fearful Admiration); Minor: Salt Star (Approval) Secrets: The Prince Resplendent hoards silver, jade, and artifacts, secretly withholding from his patrons a fraction of every haul. He maintains trapped, undead-guarded caches across the River Province, and he comes after anyone who dares loot one.

Equipment: Soulsteel articulated plate set with a hearthstone, soulsteel storm hatchets, ciphered journal, map of a First Age ruin.

Roleplaying Hints: Having dwelt in penury before becoming a scavenger lord, the Prince Resplendent places inordinate value on wealth. He raids tombs and steals artifacts — whether to serve his mentor the Mask of Winters, or hired by other patrons such as the Walker in Darkness, Nexus’ Council of Entities, or House Ragara. He circumvents combat via bluster and bribery; failing that, he flees to sites full of traps and minions. Cornered, he offers up treasure caches or his master’s secrets. Slaying him draws the Mask’s wrath.

Contraption of Bone and Sinew (5m; Simple; Instant): Any corpse can be fashioned into a trap (Exalted, p. 232) — a snare made from viscera, a ribcage bear-trap, a femur sharpened into a scything blade. Once per scene, the Prince may reveal that an enemy has fallen prey to one he’s previously set. This creates a one-time environmental hazard, difficulty 3. An injurious trap deals 3L damage; escaping a snare requires a Strength 3+, difficulty 4 feat of strength. This Charm resets when an opponent botches a roll on any physical action. ### SPELLS

The Face of Bone (5sm, 1wp; One scene): See, hear, and speak at any distance through a prepared skull of at least human size, which glows with cold blue glyphs and hovers in midair, turning as desired. Influence and perception through the skull can’t use Charms. Can’t sense or take actions with own body

while spell remains active. Taking damage or destroying the skull ends the spell. Raise the Rotting Horde (Control) (15sm, 1wp; Indefinite): Animate corpses as zombies, up to a size 2 battle group with poor Drill and Might 1. Zombies remain animated indefinitely unless exposed to sunlight for a full scene. As a control effect, the battle group has average Drill instead, or elite Drill by spending a Willpower while casting.

Summon the Shadow-Eaten (Ritual, 2wp; Instant): Summon an Essence 1-3 mortwight (Exalted, p. 505) or other monstrous ghost in a night-long ritual, rolling (Intelligence + Occult) against its Resolve to bind it for a year and a night. If this fails, roll (Wits + Occult) against its Willpower to banish it.

### The Liminal Exalted

### THE LIMINAL EXALTED

The following characters are Liminals, Chosen of the Dark Mother.

#### Dame Crimson

Background: The woman who would become Dame Crimson made a fair living on the trade route between Thorns and Nexus, guarding the merchant prince Casim Thurat’s caravan from bandits, vengeful ghosts, and other villains. She relished her return home to her bustling city, showering her many nieces and nephews with trinkets from her travels. A decade ago, Thorns fell to the Mask of Winters and she was there when the skies split asunder and the ground heaved with the behemoth Juggernaut’s emergence. She defended the walls from the howling horde of undead. They tore her to pieces.

Thurat hated to lose one of his best guards — after all, she not only protected him, she also protected the caravan’s profits. At his behest, the caravan god’s priest, Imarid, pieced her back together the best he could. Where pieces of

the woman’s body were missing, he substituted others; the city’s fall left him with plenty to choose from. Dame Crimson awoke with the

Dark Mother’s voice in her mind, singing songs

of glory.

In many ways, Dame Crimson continues the life

of her progenitor as it had been before, as the commander of the caravan’s security forces. To remain in the Mask of Winters’ good graces — and avoid his lieutenants’ scrutiny — Thurat trades obsidian, chalcedony, and soul-eaten workers to the Deathlord. Business is not only highly profitable, it also gives the caravan cover to smuggle weapons into the city and refugees out. Dame Crimson takes pride in her prowess. When wraiths threatened to expose the caravan’s deals with the living to the Mask of Winters, she dispatched them. She fended off an undead behemoth in the wild lands between Thorns and Nexus, and though it took Imarid several days to patch her up, she bears those scars with pride.

Image: Much of Dame Crimson’s body was solidly built even before her death. When he created her, the priest Imarid meticulously selected body parts that were as close to her originals as he could find — a practice he continues whenever the need arises. Though her short bobbed hair is naturally red, she dyes it a deep crimson, which gives her already pale skin an even more ghostly pallor. When she expends Essence, the whites of her eyes turn blood red, and the smell of stagnant water surrounds her.

EX3

Intimacies: Defining Principle: “I’ll protect my charges from threats both living and dead.”; Defining Tie: Imarid (Devotion); Major Principle: “There’s no shame in profiting from duty.”; Major Ties: Her Body’s Nieces and Nephews (Found Family); Casim Thurat (Duty); Minor Principle: “Sharing wealth makes one even richer.” Minor Tie: The Mask of Winters (Enmity)

Secrets: Dame Crimson has recently been reunited with one of her body’s nieces. Though she has no recollection of the young woman, Crimson saw no harm in picking up where her predecessor left off. Now, she funnels money and supplies to her when she can, intending to smuggle her newfound “family” out of Thorns when the opportunity arises. Neither of them knows that the niece’s brother is now an Abyssal in service to The Mask of Winters, one currently keeping tabs on his siblings and their mysterious good fortune. Equipment: Bonerender (soulsteel daiklave), amulet bearing the caravan god’s sigil, a suture kit Roleplaying Hints: Dame Crimson is passionate, and relishes life, eagerly rising to the challenge when a formidable enemy appears, and rarely refusing an opportunity to eat and drink well. Rather than hoard her hardearned wealth, she spends it freely, tipping generously at teahouses and regularly pausing to drop coins in beggars’ cups. She keeps lovers in every city and spoils them with extravagant gifts.

Aspect: Blood

### EXCELLENCIES

Dame Crimson may add up to (Essence) Attribute to an action at a cost of 1m per die. This increases to (Attribute + Essence) once her anima banner is at Glowing. Additionally, she may strengthen her static values by up to (Attribute / 2, round down) for a cost of 2m per die. When her anima is at Glowing, this increases to ([Attribute + Essence] / 2, round down).

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Black Soil’s Claim (4m, 1wp; Supplemental; Instant; Decisive-only): Wounds Dame Crimson leaves rot with unnatural speed. A decisive attack that deals 3+ levels of damage also causes its victim to take one die of lethal damage per turn over his next (1 + wound penalty) turns, ignoring hardness. If the victim’s wound penalty increases, so too does the duration of this effect. Undead creatures instead suffer an additional (1 + wound penalty) dice of aggravated damage added to the Liminal’s attack. Sinew Lash (3m; Supplemental; Instant) Ropes of sinew burst forth from Dame Crimson’s arms, tethering her opponent to her. This Charm supplements a grapple gambit, adding (Essence) automatic successes to the attack roll. Weeping Crimson Gaze (5m; Supplemental; Until next turn; Uniform): Dame Crimson doubles up to three dice that came up 9 on an attack roll. She may perceive and attack dematerialized undead for an instant. This Charm’s cost is reduced by Dame Crimson’s by one mote for each point of wound penalty, thanks to the easy access to blood.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Ligament Stretching Dance (3m, +1hl per two successes; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 2) Dame Crimson’s joints pop from their sockets as she bends around enemy attacks. For each health level she spends, add two successes to a Dodge roll.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Flesh Knits Closed (4m per hl; Simple; Instant) Dame Crimson’s wounds close, tendons snap back into place and torn muscle and bone reknit themselves. This Charm may be used to heal –0 and –1 health levels only.

Gravehound’s Unerring Scent (3m; Simple; Instant) Ghosts and the undead may try hiding their tracks, but Dame Crimson spots the telltale signs of their passage. Double 9s on rolls to track undead creatures.

#### Withering Vine, Shepherd Of Restless Souls

Withering Vine

Shepherd Of Restless Souls

Every spring bud holds the promise of decay; every corpse, the promise of life. Withering Vine sees the crone hiding behind the fair maiden, and the unborn heir in the old king’s loins. This inevitable cycle comforts her, even if the constant blurring of what-is and what-may-come leaves her unable to focus on the present. The Aspect of Soil lives in a far Eastern cottage with her maker, a woman who calls her “Kyo” and “beloved wife.” She likes how the maker strokes her hair and whispers forgotten tales in her ear. She doesn’t like her maker’s large, rough hands, which perfectly fit the bruises on Withering Vine’s throat. While Withering Vine ventures out into the world, a deep instinct of forgotten love and sorrow always draws her back home. Withering Vine shepherds the Unquiet. She herds lions while the sheep rest in their beds, creating boundaries to keep her ghostly flock away from mortals. She’s sympathetic to those sent to too-early graves, and only destroys dangerous undead if she has no other recourse. She ignores the lovely, dark, sing-song voice urging her to send the Unquiet to the beyond: Withering Vine will fight any who harm her charges, even other Exalts or the gods themselves. Sometimes she foresees where and when people might fight ghosts, and travels there to protect the Unquiet.

Image: Withering Vine’s skin, once a deep, rich olive, now carries the sickly greens of decay. Rot has forced her to replace parts: her left leg is a soft pink, her right foot a dark brown. She cut off her right arm and replaced it, too. Maggots fall from the corner of her right eye. Finger marks bruise her swollen neck. The Liminal wears whatever her maker dresses her in, often a simple white kimono. When she travels, her clothes grow stained and tattered.

Intimacies: Defining: “Protect the dead from the living”; Major: “Protect the living from the dead”, Maker (Fearful Love); Minor: Green Rock, erstwhile village (Protective) Secrets: The restless souls Withering Vine collects speak their murderers’ names to her, tell her where their buried treasure lies, and whisper other secrets. The Liminal

EX3

rarely acts on these — she cares not for riches, nor is it her place to punish the living — but she remembers all. Sijan’s morticians have taken note of the Shepherd of the Dead. They seek to either add her to their ranks or remove the competition, and actively recruit people to entice or force her to Sijan.

Equipment: White kimono, dirty when traveling; Restless Embrace, a Yasal Crystal (Exalted, p. 601) Roleplaying Hints: Withering Vine is withdrawn, often gazing into the distance as present and future blur together. She cannot see the past, which frustrates her. She addresses people as what they might someday be rather than what they are. Withering Vine may seek out the characters at an undead’s bequest, and serve as the Unquiet’s mouthpiece. Any attempt to destroy or banish the undead immediately earns Withering Vine’s enmity. Her windpipe is crushed, and she speaks only in whispers. She appears tranquil, but falls into a violent rage if someone threatens her flock. Withering Vine becomes docile around her maker.

Aspect: Soil

### Exigents

### EXIGENTS

The following characters are Exigents, the Chosen of individual gods throughout Creation.

#### Braze the Accursed

Background: Born into poverty in the Southern city of Paraph, blessed with good looks and charm, Braze swore he’d reach heights of luxury. Entering the priesthood, he wormed his way upward to become chief priest of Paraph’s City Mother, the god Amadeia, eventually even sharing her bed. This wasn’t enough. Ingratiating himself with Amadeia, he learned of her past indiscretions. These he sold to the Guild, attaining wealth unimaginable — and resulting in Heaven stripping Amadeia of her post so a Guild-backed god could claim Paraph. Weakened and embittered, powerless to stop Guild opium flowing into her beloved city, Amadeia found one last means of revenge. Calling in every debt owed her in the Celestial Bureaucracy, she obtained an ancient portion of the Flame of Exigence that had passed through many hands. Setting her curse upon it, she sacrificed herself to send it to her betrayer.

Image: Exalted by twisted, volatile power, Braze scarcely seems human. His face is too grotesque to look upon, his body huge and misshapen, his flesh layered with chitin and crystal. He twitches and mutters at the thousand voices whispering in his brain, uncertain which is his own. His anima banner is a reverberating cloud of spiders, jewels, and tumbling fragments of cityscapes. Intimacies: Defining: “Can anything atone for my crimes?”; Himself (Loathing); Major:

Amadeia

(Regret);

Paraph’s

new city god (Hatred); Minor:

Paraph (Homesickness); The Guild

(Bitterness)

Secrets: Braze knows a great deal

of gossip about the doings of various Southern gods and Guild merchant princes. Equipment: Tattered fragments of

clothing.

EX3

### LEGENDARY SIZE

While in Apocalyptic Arachnid Avatar form, Braze suffers no onslaught penalties from attacks from smaller opponents, unless they’re magically inflicted. Withering attacks from smaller enemies cannot Crash him unless they have at least 10 post-soak damage dice, although attackers still gain the full amount of Initiative damage dealt. Decisive attacks from smaller enemies cannot deal more than (3 + attacker’s Strength) levels of damage to him, not counting levels added by Charms or other magic.

Roleplaying Hints: Cast out by his people, rejected by his Guild patrons, Braze wanders the South seeking redemption and release. Even at his most lucid, he’s uncertain of what’s righteous. His fits of uncontrolled emotion result in violence and destruction. Despite his suffering, he’s not yet ready to die, and flees from battle if overwhelmed. What he most desires is a cure for his condition, which is probably impossible; others have played on this before, making him wary, but the right story can still entice him.

### EXCELLENCIES

Braze may add up to (Attribute) dice to an action, or (Attribute / 2) to a static value. In an urban area where Braze stunts the environment to his advantage, this increases to (Attribute + Ability) dice, or ([Attribute + Ability] / 2, round down) to a static value.

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Apocalyptic Arachnid Avatar (20m, 1wp; Reflexive; One scene): Usable only while a −4 health level is filled with damage, or at 20+ Initiative. Braze swells into a monstrous spider built from crystalline ruins — his legs are carven pillars, his body a vaulted dome whose underside is hung with a hundred glassy bells. He heals all damaged −2 health levels, gains the Legendary Size Merit and Hardness 10, and adds +4 to Strength and soak. Once per round, the first time Braze moves, is knocked down, suffers Initiative Crash, or takes decisive damage, the clangor of his bells creates a reflexive withering attack rolled against all opponents within short range, dealing a maximum of three damage to each target. He returns to normal form at end of scene, the crystalline spider-shape crumbling away. Once per story; reset by placing himself at genuine risk to uphold a Defining Intimacy. Echoing Strike Technique (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Withering-only): Braze’s afterimage mimics his movements, striking a second time. Apply the same withering attack roll twice to the target. If both attacks hit, apply the same damage roll to each attack. Once per scene; reset by defeating a nontrivial opponent. Swarm of Ruby Spiders (7m, 1ahl; Supplemental; Instant; Uniform): Upon performing a successful savage action, Braze vomits up a mass of crystal spiders onto his opponent. Treat this as an environmental hazard (3L/round, difficulty 5) lasting four rounds. Once per scene.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Crystal Exoskeleton Extrusion (5m; Reflexive; One scene): Gain +4 soak and 4 Hardness. Edge-of-Ruin Resilience (—; Permanent; Permanent): When Braze takes damage that fills his first −4 health level, he can’t lose any more health levels until his next turn ends.

Reflected Echo Body (3m, 1i; Reflexive; Instant): Braze seems to be in two places at once, gaining +2 Evasion. If an attack misses, he steals one Initiative from the attacker.

Sapphire Razor Carapace (3m, 1i; Reflexive; Instant; Counterattack, Decisive-only): When targeted by a savage, drag, or restrain action, execute a

decisive counterattack that happens before the opponent’s damage roll, replacing any wound penalty with an equal number of bonus dice.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Spider Pounce Technique (2m; Supplemental; Instant): Leap one range band horizontally or vertically; counts as the turn’s movement action. Reduce cost to 1m if used on previous turn.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Echoing Passion Pronouncement (3m; Supplemental; Instant): While inflamed by a powerful emotion, Braze adds four bonus dice to inspire actions to incite that emotion in others.

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Feeling the Earth’s Web (4m; Reflexive; Instant): Braze gains enhanced perception of vibrations. Roll (Perception + Awareness) dice to sense a concealed character coming within medium range. He can sense major vibrations (e.g., armies marching, a tyrant lizard walking, etc.) out to four miles. Lost in the Crowd (6m; Reflexive; One scene; Mute): Braze can blend unnaturally into urban crowds as though they were darkness or fog, allowing stealth rolls with no penalties.

#### Cuelebre jin-Hua, Supplier of Secret Arsenals

Cuelebre jin-Hua

Supplier of Secret Arsenals

Background: Once upon a time, world-shaking weaponry held warmongers hostage. Star-swords turned in the Heavens, ready to crash upon mustering armies, and so the world was at peace.

Ivory Shadow Smith, Eastern God of Secret Arsenals, knows such times have passed. The ash-shadow of ancient ordnance hangs heavy over Creation, and unworthy successors have laid claim to the apocalyptic weapons of a lost age. He works in secret to wrest away powerful artifacts and bury them in his armory, far from the hands of mortals and Exalted alike.

The hill-tribe of jin-Hua bandits were his favorite agents, swift saboteurs who raided outposts and depots at his prophesized instruction. Yet when his eyes were elsewhere, the jade jaws of Lookshy closed around his people. The lone survivor was Cuelebre, a dwarf-girl who’d been left at camp. Together, they prayed. Together, they were answered, and Smith reforged Cuelebre in the

furnace of the Exigence.

Chosen and god were not united long. Cuelebre saw little point hiding weapons from nations already at each other’s throats, and soon decided to sell her services. Within a matter of years, she became the Guild’s premier military trafficker, an extravagant and exuberant merchant prince whose deal-closing parties are a matter of legend to free companies across the East and South. Cuelebre sells to royalty and revolutionaries, cults and criminals. She delivers high-quality firedust to besieging forces, then smuggles plague-javelins to the entrenched enemy. Ivory Shadow Smith bemoans what has become of his Exalt, but she is unmoved. She just provides supply; demand is the ash-streaked way of the world. Image: A young dwarf woman with freckled tan skin, short-cut dark hair, and golden eyes, Cuelebre enjoys a wide variety of extravagant military dresswear when

EX3

courting clients. Even on the road she cannot resist clothing that offers luxury, comfort, and the ability to conceal a light warstrider harness.

Intimacies:

Defining:

Weaponry

(Enthusiastic

Appreciation); Major: “Blame the hilt, not the blade.”; Minor: Ivory Shadow Smith (Discomfort) Secrets:

The Mask of Winters has offered Cuelebre the chance to peruse his dark armory, seeking to build links with the living Guild. She’s on-guard for betrayal… but an honest trading relationship might be worse for Creation as a whole. Ivory Shadow Smith is aware of these overtures and hopes to intervene through Exalted allies. Equipment: A fine firewand, the cavalry saber used to behead Raol Decem, a purse bulging with notes of credit and various currencies, and several armored and covered wagons, one reinforced to bear the weight of a warstrider. Roleplaying Hints: Cuelebre is a self-indulgent showperson who genuinely enjoys extolling the virtues of her deadly wares. She’ll cheerfully take risks in return for a payday, always aiming to expand her wealth and connections or acquire artifacts of note. Of course, as a merchant who appreciates quality work and mighty weapons, she’ll just as happily pay off her enemies.

### EXCELLENCIES

Cuelebre can spend up to (Ability) motes on her Excellency, adding an equal number of dice. If she stunts an action in a way that describes somehow turning war-like materiel to her advantage, this increases to (Attribute + Ability). For example, intimidating a bandit by mocking the quality of his axe would increase the dice she could add to the roll from (Presence) to (Charisma + Presence).

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Trusted Sword Betrayal (5m; Reflexive; Instant): Once per scene after successfully disarming an opponent, Cuelebre can make a reflexive withering attack with the weapon against its former owner. This Charm can be reset by landing a decisive attack and building back to Initiative 15+.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Blunt Blade Appraisal (3m; Simple; Instant): When a character makes a threat, Cuelebre can tell if they intend to follow through, and how good they consider their chances. Magic contesting this effect goes to a roll-off against her (Perception + War), adding (Essence) automatic successes.

Devil-Cowing Demonstration (7m, 1wp;

Simple; Instant): Cuelebre expounds on a weapon’s terrible power, doubling 8s on a threaten roll. Success causes the audience to fear or desire the weapon (or both, her choice). If a character has a negative Intimacy toward a character or organization they believe to already possess this weapon, the cost to resist equals the strength of that Intimacy.

Soft-Speech Reinforcement (5m or 2m; Reflexive; Instant): Instead of comparing Appearance to Resolve when she rolls for (or is targeted by) social influence, Cuelebre compares the damage bonus of her weapon to that of the other character. If they are unarmed, she increases her bonus by a further +1. This benefit applies to all social rolls, and costs only two motes for intimidation.

### COVERT CHARMS

Emerald Sheathe Attitude (3m, +1wp; Simple; One scene): Cuelebre guises swords as ploughshares, ensuring other characters do not regard her as armed or dangerous unless she takes overt threatening action. Alert guards can notice her weapons, but still dismiss them. She can extend this illusion over a wagon-sized vehicle she is riding by spending one

### WARSTRIDER INBOUND

Ormolu is a warstrider, equal parts armor, steed, and idol of destruction. Full rules for warstriders are found in Arms of the Chosen.

Traits: Cuelebre uses the Toad’s Strength, soak, Hardness, and damage track in place of her own. She cannot directly improve these traits with magic that would improve her own, and uses her own for Charm calculations and limits.

Mobility: Ormolu offers a +1 Speed bonus on combat movement rolls and tests of speed. It can move away from human-sized foes (not battlegroups) without disengaging. Stealth rolls suffer a penalty of four successes. Defense: Within the Toad, Cuelebre cannot be targeted by decisive attacks, or take damage from environmental hazards or similar dangers. The Toad has the Legendary Size Merit (Exalted, p. 557). It cannot be grappled or knocked back by smaller enemies without magic, and is immune to poison, disease, and non-magical environmental hazards. Offense: Each turn Cuelebre can make a special reflexive attack against an enemy battlegroup, or all trivial opponents within range. This does not occupy her action. The Toad’s attacks are unblockable except by artifacts or defensive magic. It takes a −4 penalty on physical actions or Awareness rolls against human-sized targets.

#### Odara, Chosen of Ash,Captain of the Kalaho Ahn

Odara Chosen of Ash

Captain of the Kalaho Ahn

Background: A petty criminal en route to the prisons of Wavecrest, Odara found herself shipwrecked on the tiny volcanic island of Ma’o. Passing herself off as a pilgrim, she ingratiated herself with the island’s volcano-god, Ash on the Wind. But she fell in love with the god, who loved her in return.

Ash on the Wind’s chief priestess, Liliha, discovered Odara’s history — and her liaison with the god. Enraged, Liliha sent Odara to the Black Temple of Abalone, to be sacrificed to great Hamoji.

Seeing no other means

to save his lover,

Ash on the Wind

called upon the

Unconquered

Sun for deliverance. His volcano erupted, shattering Ma’o, as Odara Exalted in a blaze of volcanic fire.

Fleeing the Black Temple, Odara

united the pirate band called the

Bloodthirsty (see The Bloodthirsty,

Scourge of Wavecrest see p. XX) under her

banner to avenge herself on those who tore her from her god-lover. Seizing ships and raiding ports, she gathers the Western treasures that might otherwise enrich Wavecrest’s temples. Fire and terror linger in her wake. Image: Tall and well-built, with bronze skin and long, wavy red hair, Odara carries herself with arrogant pride. Her expressive face shows every emotion — she’s occasionally pensive, but more often cheerful, excited, or bloodthirsty. Her iconic anima depicts an erupting volcano. Intimacies: Defining: “I’ll avenge Ash on the Wind.”; Major: “I enjoy violence.”; Yalon (Friendship); The Bloodthirsty (Camaraderie); Minor: “I love treasure.”; Priests (Hatred); The Kalaho Ahn (Pride)

Secrets: Liliha lives, concealing her slick burn scars behind a perfumed veil. She seeks aid from dark powers to destroy Odara, covertly offering wealth and priestly influence. She’ll deal with any being, from the dead to the Anathema, and has already forged a bargain with a mysterious spirit that calls itself Shade.

EX3

Mad Kenzon, the fanatic who warned of the end of days on street corners and slept under the bridge most nights, arrived first. His aimless wanderings were unremarkable enough, no matter where they took him. Silver Egret, tutor to the local magistrate’s children, arrived second, shielded by her status in the prominent household. She was beyond reproach within the family, and by extension, within the community. No one dared question her personal dealings, so long as her actions didn’t endanger her young charges. Little did anyone suspect — they did.

The last to arrive was known simply as the Banker, though he rarely traded in coin. The economy of favors he ran from the back room of the local bar was far more lucrative, and far deadlier. A deep scar across his left cheek marred his weather-worn face. He claimed he’d earned it in a knife fight. That was almost the truth. It was the last remnant of the one member of their little conclave who’d fallen to the surface world’s temptations. She’d dared to dream that Creation might go on living, and for that heresy her bones lay scattered at the bottom of Bladewind Ravine, gnawed clean by the wild things that dwelt there.

Tonight, though, the Banker’s menacing face cracked into an uncharacteristic smile. “Sister!” He spread his arms wide, squeezing Silver Egret tightly to him and holding her there long enough to say what his words wouldn’t: “I’ve missed you.”

The two shared no blood, but the Last Bastion conclave had grown closer in their years away from home than any bond of family could have made them.

Silver Egret returned the embrace. “It’s good to see you well. I hope the guards were sufficiently late to find you last time around?”

The Banker nodded. “You did well. I was long gone.”

Kenzon rose from his chair by the fire. Though his rags were damp from the weather and putrid with the smells of unwashed skin, the well-dressed Banker pulled him into an embrace just the same, pressing his forehead to his companion’s. “I trust you’ve been taking care of yourself out there, my brother?” “I try,” the preacher said, “but the winters keep coming sooner and sooner. Perhaps that’s a sign on its own.” The three granted themselves a few luxurious minutes to drop their façades and bask in the warmth of familiar company before settling down to the night’s business.

“We’re nearly there,” Silver Egret said. “Siro — the magistrate’s oldest — is disillusioned with his father. He’ll come into the family’s money, once the old man finally passes.”

“How much longer?” The Banker asked. “How many more doses do you expect you’ll need?” Silver Egret thought carefully, running the calculation in her head as she tried to determine how much more of the poison it would take to carry the old man off in good time, but not suspiciously good time. “Give it another week. Maybe two. Three draughts should be plenty to finish the job.”

The Banker nodded. “You’ll have it.”

“Are you certain the boy will listen to you?” Kenzon asked. “When the time comes, I mean?” Silver Egret shrugged. “He listens to you, you know — at night, when you wander by the gates, when he doesn’t think anyone will notice. That’s about as good a sign as I could hope for. When the old man is dead, I’ll light a lantern in the east window.” The plan was the culmination of years of work. They’d been over it a thousand times. Still, it was good to repeat it once more.

“I’ll be watching for it,” said Kenzon. “We’ll bring the boy here and make our case before anyone else can sway him.”

“And,” the Banker added, “If he won’t join us and bend his fortune to Aton’s cause?” Silver Egret smiled. “The magistrate does have a second son. It’s a dangerous city, after all.”

## Chapter Five: Groups

### The Bloodthirsty, Scourge of Wavecrest

Chapter Five

Groups

5 Descending Water

I write this by candlelight. The rocking waves make my handwriting uneven, or perhaps it’s fear causing the tremor in my grip. Our captain spotted the pirate’s ship too late in the fog, recognized their flag with its bloody skull when they were close enough to catch us. Now I fear the crew is dead. We passengers have been shuffled down to the hold, to await the payment of our ransoms. I pray our letters — and thus our rescuers — arrive swiftly.

The Bloodthirsty,

Scourge of Wavecrest

Background: Vicious and brutal, the pirate band called the Bloodthirsty haunts the waters southeast of the Wavecrest Archipelago, seizing merchant vessels and raiding coastal towns. Though they often hold wealthy travelers alive for ransom, they show no such mercy to Wavecresters, for whom their mistress holds a special hatred. Their depredations strike terror across the archipelago, but House V’neef sees them as pawns in its schemes. Their sigil — a skull whose jaws drip blood — and their spiked hulls hung with their victims’ remains are rightly feared throughout the Wavecrest region. Wavecrest’s attempts to eliminate them have proven ineffectual against their numbers and secret benefactors.

The Imperial Navy is reluctant to take the fight to the pirates’ home ground, lest they turn their attention from Wavecrest. Available Navy forces would doubtless crush the Bloodthirsty in time, but the resulting distraction and casualties would leave House Peleps’ Western operations vulnerable to V’neef maneuvering. Members: The Bloodthirsty are reavers from the Seawalk Isles, an island chain southeast of Wavecrest containing Kyna Wehe, Jora Wehe, and several lesser isles. Traditionally, they limited themselves to raiding one another and unfriendly neighbors, or indulged in occasional coastal piracy. But the Exigent Odara, Chosen of Ash (p. XX) has raised her bloody-skull banner among peoples already growing eager to test the limits of an unstable Realm’s defenses, gathering dozens of ships into a single marauding fleet.

The Bloodthirsty fleet evolved from refitted fishing boats into larger vessels with a tradition of deliberately

haphazard-looking construction that’s deceptively swift and maneuverable. Their most notable quirk is the addition of great, curving spikes along the sides of their hulls, above the waterline. Meant to snag the sides of enemy ships during boarding actions, they also serve to intimidate, especially when the crews impale victims of successful raids upon them. Odara of Ash, a volcano-god’s Exigent, leads the Bloodthirsty from her flagship, the Kalaho Ahn. Her captains theoretically stand as equals, though they jockey with one another for influence over Odara and for control of the fleet’s resources. These captains include the ruthless and opportunistic Nanahu, and the dreaded ghost-blood Two Shadows.

Secrets: Despite Odara’s victories and charisma, not all Bloodthirsty favor her command. Some consider her crusade against Wavecrest misguided, and would rather turn toward less perilous targets. Others still chafe under old rivalries with fellow Bloodthirsty captains who once raided their homes. Though the need to stand together against the Imperial Navy and other maritime forces keeps Odara’s rivals in line for now, the right opportunity could unleash strife within the fleet as captains nurse old grudges.

While the Bloodthirsty remain personae non gratae in Wavecrest itself, some neighboring peoples and citystates unofficially tolerate them for their lavish spending and bribes in port, and for weakening the Realm’s influence in the region. These quietly offer havens for Bloodthirsty ships to resupply, sell stolen cargoes, and provide leave for their crews.

EX3

House V’neef secretly backs the Bloodthirsty, funneling information on Imperial Navy movements and Wavecrest shipping to the pirate captains, and offering sanctuary and trade opportunities in their satrapial port of Nansha. The Merchant Fleet hopes to use the Bloodthirsty as a weapon against Peleps in their escalating conflict for control over the West. They’ve not yet offered Odara everything she desires — total license against the priesthoods and peoples of Wavecrest — but the time for scruples is ending.

Resources: The Bloodthirsty command a dozen ships of the line with blue-water merchant traits, a score of light corsairs with large yacht traits, and several dozen scouts with fast courier traits (Exalted, p. 246). Multiple scouts

usually accompany a ship of the line or a pair of corsairs. Larger fleets assemble to take down well-defended convoys, intercept enemy naval forces, or protect pirate havens. Bloodthirsty pirates aboard a ship of the line or corsair with spikes affixed gain +1 to Threaten actions against other crews.

Crew members use Lintha reaver traits (Exalted, p. 497) with average or elite Drill depending on the captain’s skill at command. Scout and corsair crews are Size 1; a ship of the line’s crew is Size 3. Intimacies typically include “I’ll make my fortune through piracy” and Fellow Bloodthirsty (Loyalty) at Defining or Major. Ties toward Odara and/or their ship’s captains are common; their emotional tenor varies.

### NEW NAVAL STRATAGEM

Jagged Impact (Wits + Sail + Maneuverability; Cost: 15 Momentum) — Bloodthirsty ships of the line and corsairs can undertake this boarding action (Exalted, p.245). After the crew boards the enemy battle group suffers −1 to rout checks and command actions. For each threshold success on this stratagem’s roll, the captain may inflict one level of Magnitude damage to the enemy as spikes scatter or impale them.

EX3

Individual Bloodthirsty come away with sizable shares of wealth after a successful fight, which they’re quick to spend in port. Captains stash away lockboxes of treasure for emergencies, and many have Resources 1-3 in non-maritime investments.

Roleplaying Hints: Bloodthirsty ships try to outnumber their opposition, swarming one target at a time and prioritizing boarding actions. In melee, they fight fiercely, showing no quarter unless an individual seems valuable — whether for ransom, useful skills, or carnal appeal. On shore leave, they tend toward bellicosity; they’re prone to brawling and bravado, as well as muggings and other petty crime.

Arms Master

The arms master’s responsibilities include drilling the crew in combat techniques and commanding them in battle. She’s often the most skilled fighter onboard, and leads from the front to repel boarders or seize enemy ships. Commercial vessels’ arms masters also lock up weapons between engagements to avoid fights and mutiny, but pirates rarely enforce such rules.

Ship’s Pilot

While the captain gives orders and commands the ship as a whole, the pilot is responsible for navigation and steering. (On smaller ships, captain and pilot may be one and the same). She holds a great deal of authority, effectively acting as second-in-command. This also makes her a prime target during naval engagements, so a pilot must be skilled in her own defense.

As talented pilots are rare, pirates seize them from captured vessels and press them into service. Though carefully watched at first, most are nonetheless reliable; they know enemy navies will treat them as any other pirate despite their origins and intentions.

Nanahu, Captain of the Wa Wakahi

Born in a Kyna Wehe fishing village, Nanahu turned first to sailing and then to piracy, where his fierce demeanor and violent temperament stood him in good stead. When the Wa Wakahi’s previous captain died in battle, he roused the crew to his support and seized the captaincy. While Nanahu approves of Odara’s personal strength and tactical acumen, he fears her vendetta against Wavecrest may embroil the Bloodthirsty in an unwinnable, unprofitable war with the Imperial Navy; or worse, that they’ll become tools of the Merchant Fleet.

Nanahu is squat and powerfully built, with blue-green hair and beard. An encounter with a fire projector turned the left side of his face into a mass of burn scars, making his expression all the more ferocious.

Two Shadows,

Captain of the Nightmare

Olo the Scorpion, Wary Fence

The hard-goods dealer Olo spends any given day picking through new goods in Port Ahisa’s bazaar. She moves slowly; a wooden stump replaces her missing left leg, and her once-muscular frame now runs to fat. Her neighbors would be astonished to see her on moonless nights, nimbly descending cliffside paths to the hidden cove where she purchases pirated cargoes.

She maintains a walled, guarded warehouse complex at the edge of town. When not out on business, she rarely stirs herself from the office where she cooks her books. She keeps a crossbow by her desk, and her clothing bristles with hidden knives; she trusts no one, not even her own staff. Once a pirate herself in the now-defunct Cracked Skull Sisterhood of Jora Wehe until its destruction by Kyna Wehe fleets 20 years ago, Olo retains many contacts amid the Bloodthirsty beyond her mercantile relationships. She knows much regarding their activities and plans.

After perishing in a storm, a fisherman returned home as a ghost to visit his wife one last time. Nine months later, Two Shadows was born. Drawn to the sea, she became a sailor and a pirate. Eventually caught by Wavecrest’s navy, she was slated for execution in Abalone before a serendipitous Bloodthirsty raid freed her. Two Shadows is a tall, lean, pale woman in her 30s, with a white streak through her green-black hair. Her crew fears the deathly magic of her birthright, even as they admire her cold, calculated brutality. Though aloof, Odara’s fiery presence has warmed something within her for the first time, turning her aside from pure profit to serve the pirate queen’s crusade.

Intimacies: Defining: “Unlike people, money will never betray you.”; Major: Survivors of the Cracked Skull Sisterhood (Loyalty); Kyna Wehe’s chieftains (Hatred); Minor: “Better to kill than to be killed.”; The Bloodthirsty (Loyalty)

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Life-Drinking Blade (3m, 1i; Supplemental; Instant; Dual; Essence 1): Gain +2 to an attack roll. Gain +1 to post-soak withering damage or to raw decisive damage.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Clarity-of-Death Defense (2m; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 1): Ignore one point of onslaught penalty.

Two Shadows can perceive and physically interact with ghosts and other immaterial undead, who can likewise interact with her even without materializing. These effects don’t apply to other immaterial spirits.

Kraken’s Shroud (Black Jade Reinforced Buff Jacket, Artifact •••)

Two Shadows seized this gray sharkskin and black jade coat from an outcaste warrior-priest of Siakal.

Evocations of Kraken’s Shroud

### Children of the Weaver

Background: While the Contagion raged across Creation, its awful ripples shook the very threads of the Loom of Fate. One violent tremor knocked a Pattern Spider loose from its place on the tapestry, and the creature fell to earth. A group of mortals found the lost, injured spider, brought it home to their town, and nursed it back to health as best they could. When it recovered, rather than returning to Heaven, the spider stayed with the people. Weavers noticed tiny, intricate patterns the spider had spun onto their looms. They sold the fabric and the town grew prosperous. The people worshiped the spider as a small god, and in return it brought them good fortune and prosperity.

Then it began to weave people of its own: human-like creatures spun from its silk, who wandered the town observing its mortal inhabitants. They watched without comment as the people went about their daily tasks, neither helping nor hindering... at least not in any immediately obvious manner. Then the blacksmith, whose small kindnesses were known throughout the town, survived an accident at the forge that should have killed her. The tailor, whose greed was no secret, awoke one morning to find his fabrics moth-eaten and moldy, his needles bent. Other shifts of fate followed, with one common thread connecting them: those who were rewarded or punished had all been watched by the spider’s strange children.

Over the years, groups of the spider’s progeny have ventured out into the wider world, to observe other mortals’ lives. Everywhere they go, they judge the actions of those they watch, and peer a little ways ahead on the tapestry to tweak a strand of their target’s fate here, a strand there, depending on the person’s deeds. They aren’t perfect arbiters of good and evil. The pattern spider modeled its children on the humans that saved it all those centuries ago, and they’re therefore susceptible to flattery and deception, just like other humans. They can be persuaded to change the fate of an awful person for the better, or a kindly person for the worse. Image: From a distance, the Children of the Weaver look human. They’re all genders and sizes, depending on the pattern spider’s whims and inspirations the day it spun out their lives. Up close, their skin has a silvery sheen. From even closer, observers can see the individual stitches that make up their flesh. Their hair is fine-spun wire in varying metallic hues. The embroidery on their clothing is impeccable; the keen-eyed might notice subtle changes in the scenes over the course of an evening. Intimacies: Defining: “Fortune is a gift; it’s my duty to bestow or rescind it.” Major Tie: The Weaver (Reverence); Minor: “Some courses require a bit of correcting.” Typical Individuals: Though they bear a common progenitor, once the Children of the Weaver leave the web,

EX3

they quickly become individuals. Their opinions and actions form based on the people they observe and the glimpses ahead on the tapestry they seek out. The Weaver’s Children don’t have a strict hierarchy. Most decisions that need to be made are made as a group, with members discussing how their various options will affect the near future and coming to a consensus. Humans fascinate the Weaver’s Children. They see how the person’s choices affect her section of the tapestry, how those decisions tug on threads surrounding hers, and so on. Most feel they’re superior to humans and view themselves as a guiding force in the lives of the people they choose to “help.” Some consider themselves philanthropists, improving the fates of as many of the deserving as they can. Others are judges, meting out misery to make malefactors repent their crimes. Still others view humans as an interesting experiment, tweaking their subjects’ threads at random to see what happens. Secrets: After several outside sources attempted to kidnap or kill the pattern spider, it has retreated to a hidden lair outside the town that saved it. The Children are bound to their parent by fate, and thus know its whereabouts.

One of the Getimian Exalted is aware of the Children and watches their behavior with interest. The Children are of the Loom of Fate, yet removed from it — not in the same way Rakan Thulio and the Getimians are, but perhaps the Children of the Weaver could be of use in the war against Heaven. A small group of the Children has begun weaving its own miniature Loom of Fate. While it’s nowhere near potent enough to compete with the original in Heaven, it chronicles the lives of a small village the Children have co-opted to see where their threads will take them. The villagers are unaware of this, and accept the strange anomalies that result as everyday occurrences.

Resources: Even Children who depart for the other side of Creation consider the pattern spider’s lair their home, and the human town nearby their refuge. The townspeople still revere and worship the Children, and in return receive the Children’s blessings. They will also, if the Children or the spider are threatened, rally to protect their benefactors. They are mostly civilians, so a battle group composed of townspeople has poor Drill, but the presence of the Children gives them Might 1. (Treat them as militia, Exalted, p. 496.)

Over the years, Children of the Weaver have nudged the fates of politicians, bandit queens, entertainers, and

EX3

### SNARLS IN THE WEAVE

As good as they are at altering fates, the changes the Children make can create strange glitches in the pattern. After all, they’ve never touched the Loom of Fate itself, never seen the patterns of not just lives, but nations and generations spun out, never learned how to properly keep the threads from dropping or twisting or snagging. This leads to odd occurrences when a Child isn’t careful, or pushes fate too far. Anomalies run from small aberrations — a coin that always lands on heads — to the unignorable — a man dodging a hail of arrows, a house appearing fully built on an empty lot, an army marching across the sky. Left unchecked, these glitches can grow extremely dangerous. Sometimes, they attract Sidereal attention. Others, there’s not enough time for the problem to wend its way through the Celestial Bureaucracy, and needs to be dealt with right away, by whatever mortals and Exalts are close by.

guild members. They’re careful not to meddle with the brightly shining threads of people with great destinies, lest they attract the notice of the Sidereal Exalted, instead focusing on those whose rise ends somewhere in the middle of the ranks. Still, it’s no small thing to have the magistrate of a minor satrapy in one’s debt, or to be owed a favor by a member of the Guild operating out of Nexus. This grants them Contacts 1 or 3 in a given organization, at the Storyteller’s discretion. Roleplaying Hints: The Children have little need for money or material goods. They eat and sleep because it’s interesting and pleasurable, not for sustenance. Yet, they like a good meal and a soft bed, and therefore offer themselves for hire to those looking to change their fortunes... or the fortunes of others. Discreet in their negotiations, they take those jobs that interest them or stir their senses of justice or self-importance. If a Child dies, the changes she made remain in place. Home in its lair, the Weaver feels the loss and spins out a new child to take its place.

Should the characters go to the source and attempt to destroy the pattern spider, both Children and townspeople rush to its defense. Though succeeding in killing it or convincing it to return to the Loom may put the characters in the good graces of the Sidereals, they earn the enmity of both the townspeople and any of the Weaver’s Children who remain.

### OFFENSIVE CHARMS

Inevitable Strike Technique (5m; Simple; Instant; Essence 2): The Child considers several possible attacks in the space of a heartbeat, and knows which one will land on his opponent. Discarding the others, he chooses the one that is destined. On a successful attack, the Child ignores her target’s Soak.

### DEFENSIVE CHARMS

Blade Catching Method (3m; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 1): The Weaver’s Child knows where her opponent’s blade will land, and brings her own weapon up to turn the blow aside. Once per round, she may use this Charm to double her Parry.

### MOBILITY CHARMS

Advantageous Positioning (3m; Reflexive; Instant; Essence 2): When a Child’s Initiative is lower than her enemy’s, she finds the best place to stand within his range band and plants herself there, gaining the upper hand. Her Initiative temporarily increases to one higher than her target’s, then reverts to its former value at the end of the round. This Charm may be used once per scene.

### SOCIAL CHARMS

Study the Tapestry (5m; Simple; Instant; Essence 2): Each person’s life contributes to the shape of the pattern. A Weaver’s Child may look back along her target’s thread, spotting significant moments and weighing their impact on the lives of others.

### The Darienites of Yane,Children of Green and Gold

### MISCELLANEOUS CHARMS

Common Threads (3m; Simple; Instant; Essence 1): The Weaver’s Child scrutinizes a person or a location and sees the way other people or objects interconnect. She understands the relationships between his target and people nearby, and whether anything in the immediate area is tied to any of that person’s Intimacies.

Fortune Comes Calling (10m; Simple; Instant; Essence 2): The Child plucks at the threads of her target’s future, nudging upcoming situations to fit the punishment or reward the Child envisions for that person. She gains three bonus dice on an Alter Fate roll. The Child can only make changes for events occurring over the next ten days, and the changes must be predicated on events that are already in motion.

Too many adjustments in quick succession, or two Children working at odds with one another, may tangle the threads of fate in dangerous or disastrous ways.

Knotted Silk Meditation (10m, 1wp; Simple; Instant; Essence 2): The Weaver’s Child tugs two targets’ threads closer together. Over the next week, the targets’ paths cross often, and the two become aware of one another’s presence. One might have information or resources the other needs, or both might be in search of the same treasure. Whether the Weaver’s Child intends them to become great friends or bitter enemies, their fates are, at least temporarily, intertwined.

Both people must be in the same general area (within medium range) when this Charm is activated.

The Darienites of Yane,

Children of Green and Gold

Background: This demon cult traces its origins to Darien Nightbreaker, a Solar Anathema whose sorceries troubled the early Realm. Once rampant throughout the South, centuries of Immaculate effort have reduced it to a shadow of its former self. Only a few scattered cells linger, but their philosophy remains potent, ready to blossom anew should a Solar show her face. The Darienite creed venerates Creation’s Unconquered Sun and Hell’s green sun as brother deities who chained the ravening Elemental Dragons at time’s dawn, and sees demons and Solar Anathema alike as champions against Dragon-Blooded tyrants. This offers spiritual strength to those suffering under the Realm’s yoke. Where the Realm’s grip is strong, the Darienites’ green-and-gold sunburst is rarely seen. But when sedition grows, the sunburst graffito appears as a mark of

resistance. Should a Solar Anathema or demon-blooded hero arise, cult members sew it upon their garments to mark themselves as followers, rallying to the sun-blessed. Darienite congregations typically form in Southern urban slums. There, a proselyte — often an escapee from Immaculate purges — can establish a following among the poorest, most downtrodden commoners without drawing attention from local monks.

In some congregations, the Twin Suns’ word spreads like wildfire; such groups flare into rebellion, only to be stamped out. Other congregations grow slowly, setting down roots, raising children and grandchildren in the secret faith. These take advantage of cult ties — and, sometimes, infernal blessings — to grow strong, rich, and powerful, sharing their bounty with their coreligionists.

### THE CITY OF YANE

Commonly known as the capital of the Varang Confederacy, this coastal city-state doesn’t rule its compatriots. Neither is it the largest, richest, or most politically powerful of the Varang city-states. Rather, it’s where the cities’ representatives meet to discuss matters of import to the Confederacy as a whole. Like other Varang city-states, Yane stands on an artificial hill, the result of centuries of razing old brickwork structures to build new ones atop the debris. Lost chambers and catacombs wend beneath the city streets, where outcasts, criminals, and cultists — such as the Darienites — gather with caution, watchful for unquiet dead and forgotten spirits.

EX3

One such deep-rooted branch of the cult exists in the Varangian port of Yane. When the Solar Anathema Fehim Three Devils (p. XX) arose in that city, the Darienites reached out to him.

Members: Most of Yane’s Darienites are low-sphere or sphereless citizens. The cult preaches that Hell’s dying stars judge all mortals to be equal, offering a sense of pride. It also provides a network for information and mutual aid. Although the cult’s handful of high-sphere members find some value in this, they’re drawn more by the cult’s rejection of the Realm’s Dragon-Blooded, whose authority and prestige supersedes their own. Yane contains dozens of small congregations, each with no more than a score of members led by a priest. They meet monthly on the night of the new moon in cellars beneath homes and businesses, or in the labyrinth of buried chambers beneath the city. Representatives of each congregation come to a great gathering during Calibration. Otherwise, religious devotion is largely private and personal. The cult’s notable figures include Old Rahenna, a widely respected elder in Yane’s sphereless community; the master thief Ghost Lizard; the constabulary officer

Sadiya, who uses her position to protect the cult; and Tourmaline, the cult’s masked high priest, who appears only at Calibration gatherings.

Secrets: Tourmaline isn’t the true leader of Yane’s Darienites. She reports to the cult’s inner circle: the Lashari family, a wealthy merchant clan that traces its descent back to Darien Nightbreaker, and whose elders study sorcery and traffic with demons. Mistrusting the Anathema Fehim — whose demon patron is a rival to the family’s own infernal sponsors — the Lashari haven’t revealed their existence to him. Resources: To better conceal themselves, Yane’s Darienites eschew distinctive marks and equipage. Even their scriptures are oral rather than written. Their greatest resources are one another. They share information, provide professional contacts, and support one another through hard times.

The Lashari family commands significant wealth and influence. It applies this carefully when covering up the outer cult’s activities, lest that lead Immaculate investigators back to its own. The Lashari also possess occult resources — sorcery, artifacts, and demon allies.

EX3

### TYPICAL CONGREGANTS

### THE LASHARI FAMILY

Most of Yane’s Darienites are ordinary folk — laborers, housekeepers, clerks, tinkers, streetwalkers, street-sweepers, ragpickers, and the like. They’re usually noncombatants (Exalted, p. 208). Typical Intimacies include “I follow the Darienite creed” and Fellow Darienites (Loyalty) at Defining or Major, and Demons and Anathema (Reverence) at Major or Minor.

An ancient family, the Lasharis maintain a palatial estate near Yane’s heart. The stars have graced them with many puissant astrologers, gifted chirurgeons, and master merchants; few families match their wealth and prestige.

Their role in the Darienite cult remains secret. For centuries, they’ve worshipped the demon Alveua (Exalted, p.527). Once each generation, a Lashari sorcerer calls Alveua forth, that she might lead pious elderly Lasharis out of death’s arms to her forge of night.

A congregation forced to fight forms a Size 1 or 2 battle group of militia (Exalted, p.496) with poor Drill.

Many artifacts forged from Lashari elders have returned to the family. Family sorcerers have long dreamed of using them in a grand working, opening the way for one of Hell’s princes to walk the world unbound. This seems impossible, yet they persist.

Roleplaying Hints: The cult currently remains underground, its members trying to avoid attention. Individuals will aid Fehim and Solar PCs in small ways, supplying useful information, conveying messages, or offering concealment from constables and the Wyld Hunt. Few stick their necks out openly. Only once the Anathema gain a strong enough position in the city to move openly will the Darienites reveal themselves. Seeing Anathema as demon princes to be worshiped, they’ll place themselves at the Solars’ disposal, acting as enforcers and priests in ways they think demons would like them to. They’ll be eager to place themselves above the city’s former high-sphere citizens, and to castigate Immaculate unbelievers.

Intimacies: Defining: Yane’s Darienites (Devotion); Major: “Whatever our spheres, we are all equal.”; Herself (Pride); The cult’s inner circle (Obedience); Minor: “I find joy in performance.”; “Those who abuse high station are contemptible.”; Demons and Anathema (Reverence)

Tourmaline, Darienite High Priest

Once she was Night Jasmine, queen of the Yane theater, with countless high-sphere admirers. But her youth is long past. She still graces the stage as a character actor, but much of her life is devoted to her greatest role: the black-cloaked, green-and-gold-masked Tourmaline, head of Yane’s Darienites.

A true believer, Tourmaline dedicates herself to the cult’s betterment. She’s memorized the entire Darienite scripture, as well as the faces and names of every lesser priest. Her sermons spellbind listeners as effectively as her youthful performances. Her eye for character makes her a most effective proselyte.

She occasionally meets with likewise-disguised members of the cult’s inner circle, who support her efforts with silver and warnings of Immaculate and constabulary investigations. But they’ve underestimated her keen senses and memory; she recognizes the elder Lasharis’ voices and movements from high-sphere entertainments decades ago.

Ayabas Lashari, Swordbearer

Despite his peaceful temperament, Ayabas — a prestigious chirurgeon’s third son — was assigned to the military sphere. A mid-ranking officer in his late twenties, he’s responsible for guard postings at Yane’s docks, where he arranges for Darienites to be on watch should the cult need to smuggle materiel or people in and out. (In practice, such smuggling usually serves the Lasharis’ financial interests, not the cult as a whole.)

EX3

Secretly, Ayabas is one of the family’s champions, bearing one of its ancestor blades. Twice he’s cloaked and masked himself to take up the daiklave and slay an enemy of the Lashari family, the sword’s hunger burning through his veins. He hopes never to do so again. Intimacies: Defining: Family (Loyalty); Major: “I have no wish to harm others.”; Ashar (Loathing); Enemies of the Lashari (Hatred); Minor: “I am a worthy Varangian officer.”; “I respect the Darienite creed.”

Ashar (Nightforged

Jevan Lashari, Infernalist Sorceress
